Every booking flow lists a person already booked on a day as an orange row only when the new booking's times actually overlap an existing booking that day (`slotInterval` intervals, `intervalsOverlap`; touching times do not clash) — My Schedule self-booking included (it used to warn for any booking that day). 1.23.0: the check is `bookingRequestClashIds({ request, existing, day })` — the existing bookings (labour or manager schedule) whose `slotInterval` overlaps the booking being made, both read under the working hours in effect on the **booked** day (never today's: pass the policy state), an overnight booking from the day before reaching that morning, cancelled bookings never; every booking flow uses it — the Schedule wizard, Book labour, the Daily Overview booking sheet, Quick Add and booking edit on web (1.23.1), Select Operatives on iOS — and `bookingLeaveConflicts` reads each booked day's working hours the same way; a weekend day's named slot is that day's own window (`bookingStandardDayOn`). A manager or admin who is double-booked may book anyway: the orange row is ticked (keep) or ✕ (drop) like the annual-leave rows, and the overlap is then a Medium manager clash warning (`managerClashGroups`).

**Excluded users** are a per-user, all-or-nothing exemption from unbooked-labour warnings, for people who are never bookable (office PAYE staff, directors). Temporarily unavailable operatives use the **inactive** flag instead.

Display order (Oldest/Newest) is a per-device preference (`sortWarningsByDate`).

## 6. Daily Overview

One entry per day listing booked people (with project or small work), unbooked people (`unbookedLabourRowsOnDay`; on a Saturday or Sunday the "Hours to cover" rule of §4.6 / §5) and people on approved annual leave. It respects the organisation working hours in effect on the day shown (`effectivePayrollTimePolicy`, history first, 1.19.0), including that weekend day's own lunch break, the fixed working week, include-weekends, exclusions and active/inactive status. Hours (1.20.2) are the pay rule's for the day shown (`payrollHoursResult` under the policy in effect that day): each booking row shows its times ("Custom 07:30–14:00") and `bookingHoursLabel(result).label` — the paid hours with the multiplier sum when hours are at a multiplier (Saturday 07:30–14:00 on an 8h set window → "8h"; Sunday 07:30–14:00 every hour × 2 → "13h (6.5h × 2)"; weekday 07:30–18:00 with 2h at 1.5 → "11h (8h + 2h × 1.5)"), never the clock duration; the day's total is the sum of `totalPaidHours`, split into standard hours (`standardHours`) and hours at the multiplier (`outsideHours`). Before 1.20.2 the rows showed clock hours worked (`workedHours`), so a Saturday or Sunday 07:30–14:00 read 6.5h on every list. 1.29.0: a booking with its own overtime rate (`otMultiplierOverride`, §25) shows that rate in its label ("9.5h (7.25h + 2h × 1.5)" becomes "10.5h (7.25h + 2h × 2)"); without one, the organisation's rate as before. A Saturday or Sunday booking shows the custom hours entered (1.13.0, §4.3); a stored weekend FULL DAY / AM / PM shows as stored. It is identical on every platform for the same day and viewer. **Who is shown** (1.33.0, owner decision 5 Nov, Q-D1 agreed 6 Nov): `dailyOverviewScopeFor({ viewer, roster, people, operatives, enforced })` → `DailyOverviewScope` (the `WarningScope` shape with no job part) and `isDailyOverviewRowInScope(scope, { personIds, email, subcontractor })`. Admins and super admins outside operative mode see every person and every subcontractor booking. A manager — whatever their Projects / Small works toggles — sees only themselves and their reports (the people whose line managers include them), under every id and on their email: their booked rows, leave, unbooked people (`unbookedLabourRowsOnDay` with this scope, so the list equals their Book labour list) and the day's hours and counts, which are taken from those rows only. A non-report booked on a job they manage, and subcontractor bookings, are not on a manager's Daily Overview (they still are in the job's scheduling). The Home team panels and the Home on-site / on-leave figures (`homeOverviewMetrics` `scope`, §23) follow the same scope. Before 1.33.0 a manager saw everyone booked and only their unbooked list was scoped.

**Booked cover for one person on a day** (1.22.0) is `managerCoverageHours({ bookings, dayKey, policy, timeZone })` → `{ paidDayHours, paidHours, blocks, requiredHours, coveredHours, missingHours, covered }`, over all their labour and manager site bookings that day (every id they go by). `covered` is `standardDayCoverage` — the Daily Overview and unbooked-labour rule — and decides who Book labour still offers on both apps. `paidHours` merges the clock intervals (`slotInterval`; overlaps count once) and prices each merged block once with `payrollHoursResult` under the policy in effect that day (hours worked, never multiplied; the unpaid break off only where a block covers the break window); a FULL DAY with no clock times, or a block spanning the standard day, is `weekdayPaidHours`. Before 1.22.0 iOS (`ManagerScheduleInterval.combinedPaidBookedHours`) took the standard break off any partly booked day and Book labour on iOS compared those hours with the paid day, while web used `standardDayCoverage`.

## 7. Weekly Report

**Admins only** (1.32.0, owner decision 5 Nov): the Weekly report is opened only by admins and super admins (outside operative mode; an admin has none) — `canOpenWeeklyReport(user)`, read from the level, so a promotion gives it and a demotion takes it away. The `weeklyReports` toggle is admin-only (`ADMIN_ONLY_PERMISSION_FIELDS`): locked on for admins, never offered to managers or operatives on Add User, Edit User or setup, and a manager's stored `weeklyReports: true` reads off and is never rewritten. Every way in — menu, Home tile and quick action, help links, a deep link and the page itself — asks `canOpenWeeklyReport`; a direct URL shows no access. Managers with the toggle on before 1.32.0 lose the report. No security rule depends on it (the report is built on the device from data the person already reads and is never stored).

Generated live for any chosen date range from current bookings, Daily Overview, warnings and timesheets. Leave days in the range are `leaveDaysInRange(record, range, timeZone, nonWorkingDayKeys)` (1.9.0, on `leaveRecordDayKeys`; a half day counts 0.5) on every platform. Contents: hours, overtime, warnings including approved clashes, totals. 1.19.0: every pay row, the timesheet feed and each row's day units (the standard day and overtime rate) use the working hours in effect on the row's own day (the whole policy state), and rows on days with different rules are separate rows, never averaged; unbooked-labour warnings read each day under its own rules (§5); signed lines keep their signed amounts. The PDF, XLSX and HTML exports show the same rows. A warning counts as open only while `isWarningActive` over `resolvedWarningKeys` (1.10.0): a dismissed clash is in neither the open warnings nor the approved clashes; approved clashes (`isClashApproved` / `isClashGroupApproved`) are listed separately. Reports are **not stored** and **never write** (1.10.0): a signed sheet with no `weeklyReportOverride` is summarised in memory for the report only — the report never writes anyone's timesheet (`syncWriteAllowed('weeklyReport')` is false). The people in the timesheet feed are `timesheetParticipant(user, day)` (§8). **The approved-timesheet feed** (1.22.0) is `weeklyReportTimesheetFeed({ weeks, people, range, timeZone })` on every platform (`mergeTimesheetFeedWeeks`, `timesheetFeedCovers`, `timesheetFeedLabourLines`, `timesheetFeedMoneyLines`): approved weeks of accounts that share an email (roster email, without case; else the user id) and the same week start are one person's week — the first week keeps its fields and its lines are the union by identity (a booking line by `bookingId`, else its day, place, job, hours, days, amount, overtime flag and details; a money line by `id`, else its day, job, title, amount and details; each identity as many times as any one week has it), so a booking both accounts carry counts once and hours on only one account stay; a person's live bookings on a day inside an approved week (organisation-zone days, inclusive) are replaced by that week's lines; labour lines in the range count except declined ones, price work and expenses except declined ones and amounts of 0.0001 or less. Behaviour is unchanged from web 1.21; iOS kept its own copy until 1.22.0. **PAYE people** (1.29.0, owner decision): a PAYE person's days (`weeklyReportPayeDay(person, dayKey)`, effective-dated `employmentTypeOnDay`) leave the normal hours and pay rows; instead a **PAYE overtime** section lists, per PAYE person per job, their overtime hours in the range — name, job number, overtime hours, e.g. "Sam · J-1042 · 1.5h" (`weeklyReportPayeOvertime({ people, bookings, organization, timeZone, range })`, the same lines as the Overtime screen, §25). **1.32.0 (owner decision 4 Nov):** rows are per person, job and multiplier and show the multiplier — "2h at ×1.5 = 3h" (`overtimeMultiplierText`; with an unpaid break "6.5h at ×2 − 0.5h break = 12.5h") — and a row whose bookings carry their own rate different from the organisation's is marked "changed from organisation rate (×1.5)" (`changedFromOrganization`, `OVERTIME_RATE_CHANGED_MARK`). Each person's lines are matched under `overtimePersonIds`. **Pay on the weekly report** (1.32.0, owner decisions 5 Nov, review fix r274): the Pay Summary on screen and in every export (PDF, HTML, XLSX) shows each person's rate and pay only when `canViewPersonPay` allows the viewer (default context — the weekly report is not a timesheet review): admins every person, a manager with the Operatives toggle operatives, everyone themself. Anyone else sees the hours with the rate and pay cells `PAY_HIDDEN_CELL_LABEL` ("—"), and the grand total is over the visible rows only with `PAY_HIDDEN_NOTE` (`reportPayForViewer(rows, { viewer, roster })` → `{ rows, grandTotal, hiddenPeople }`); a row with no known person is shown to admins only. Price work and expense amounts in their own sections are claims, not rates, and are unchanged — always live from the bookings, whatever the lines' review state; hours only. A report is produced and downloaded (PDF/XLSX/HTML or the share sheet) and that is all. Producing a report again for the same range reflects any changes made since.

## 8. Timesheets and employment profile

**Profile fields** on `users/{uid}`: employment status (`employmentType`: PAYE or self-employed), pay basis (`payBasis`: day or hourly), `dayRate`, `hourlyRate`. A rate save writes exactly one basis and removes the other; both apps show `PAY_BASIS_SAVE_NOTE` ("Pay is a day rate or an hourly rate, not both. Saving a day rate removes the hourly rate, and saving an hourly rate removes the day rate.") with `PAY_BASIS_DAY_HELP` / `PAY_BASIS_HOURLY_HELP` under the pay fields (1.11.0; iOS copy that said both rates are kept was wrong). A PAYE person's rate is kept for days they are self-employed and shown with `PAYE_RATE_NOT_USED_NOTE`. The same applies to roster rows: an `operatives` or `managers` row with `payBasis: hourly` is hourly (`parseOperativeDocument`, `parseManagerDocument`). `timesheetsEnabled` is still read and written (`defaultTimesheetsEnabled` when not stored) but, from 1.11.0, no longer decides who has a timesheet: only self-employed days do (below). Annual leave fields not stored on the user (`annualLeaveDaysPerYear`, `annualLeaveYearStartMonth`, `annualLeaveYearEndMonth`, `annualLeaveCarriesOver`) take the organisation `annualLeaveDefaults` (§4.4), never a platform constant (`parseUserDocument` with `annualLeaveDefaults`); a save never writes the default onto the user.

**Effective dating.** Status and rate are both effective-dated; a change asks when it takes effect. Status: `employmentTypeTransitionFrom` / `employmentTypeEffectiveAt` (`applyEmploymentTypeChange`, `employmentTypeOnDay`). Rate: `organizations/{orgId}/operativeDayRateHistory` rows `{ userId, operativeId, dayRate, payBasis, effectiveAt, createdAt }`, built by `payRateHistoryWriteFields`: `effectiveAt` is midnight of the effective day in the organisation zone (never London unless that is the organisation's zone, never the device zone). Clearing a rate writes a row `{ dayRate: null, payBasis: null, effectiveAt }`, meaning "rate not set from that day". History is never rewritten. **A pay change** (1.10.0) writes its rows with `payRateChangeWrites({ previousProfile, nextProfile, history, effectiveDayKey, seedEffective })`: nothing when the amount and basis are unchanged; a seed row of the previous rate from the account's creation only when the person's history loaded and is empty; then the new row. The rows are written in the same batch as the profile (`users/{uid}`), never as a best-effort second write; when the history did not load the save is refused (`history_not_loaded`, `PAY_HISTORY_NOT_LOADED_MESSAGE`) instead of treating it as empty.

**Rate on a day** (`payRateOnDay`; 1.10.0: given `person` and `roster`, history rows match every id of the person — `personIdSet`: own id, former ids, same-email ids — so each app passes the whole history): a PAYE day has rate 0 (the line keeps its hours and is flagged PAYE). Otherwise the rate is the latest history row for the person whose effective day (organisation zone) is on or before the day; an explicit 0 is a real rate. If the live profile is hourly, an hourly row stays in force until a day-rate row falls on a later day. A "rate cleared" row in force means "rate not set" (it ends an hourly row too). With no history row the live profile applies (`users/{uid}`, then the operative row). With nothing set the rate is "rate not set".

**Pay rules.**
- **PAYE has no timesheet** (1.11.0, owner decisions round 3). A PAYE day produces no timesheet line, no pay and no invoice row; it is still a booking for warnings, Daily Overview and Weekly Report hours (where its pay cell reads PAYE, `reportPayCell`). The days that count on a person's sheet for a period are `timesheetDaysForPerson(user, period, timeZone)` — every organisation-zone day of the period on which they are self-employed (`employmentTypeOnDay`, effective-dated); `showsMyTimesheets(user, period, timeZone)` is whether that list is not empty. A person sees **My Timesheets** for a period only then, gets the submission reminder only then, and their owner sheet, its invoice (`timesheetInvoiceRows` with `timesheetDays`: labour lines, price work and expenses dated on another day are left out) and the User Timesheets row for that period exist only then. A PAYE manager or admin still reviews other people's timesheets (User Timesheets is `canReviewUserTimesheets`, not this). PAYE overtime is §25 (1.29.0): it is never a timesheet line and never money; PAYE price work and expenses are §26 (1.30.0): not timesheet lines, no line-manager approval, the person's own report. `isInvoiceLine` still drops a line flagged PAYE (older data).
- Self-employed days pay the effective day or hourly rate.
- Example: PAYE → self-employed changed on Tuesday, effective Wednesday: the sheet for that period shows Wednesday onwards only; Monday and Tuesday are not on it.
- **An unpaid break is never multiplied** (1.20.3, owner decision, final): on hours at a multiplier (weekend every hour at N×, or a set-window day's outside hours) the hours are multiplied first and the break comes off as plain hours — Sunday 07:30–14:00 every hour × 2 with 30 min unpaid = 13 − 0.5 = 12.5h (§4.3, `payrollHoursResult`). Booking rows show it as "12.5h (6.5h × 2 − 0.5h break)" (`bookingHoursLabel`); (1.20.4) timesheet lines, invoices, the weekly report and Daily Overview show "6.5h × 2 − 0.5h break = 12.5h" (`multipliedHoursText` over `payrollMultipliedHours`; a signed line's stored text is read back with `parseMultipliedHoursText`), never the back-calculated base hours ("6.25h × 2"), and the line's pay is unchanged.
- **Overlapping bookings are each paid** and never merged. An overlap is a warning managers should have caught; on the timesheet each overlapping line is shown in amber for the person and their line manager with "Overlaps another booking — check with your manager" (`overlappingBookingIds`, `OVERLAPPING_BOOKING_NOTE`). Times that only touch do not overlap. A Full day, AM or PM line is the standard day in effect on that line's own day (1.23.0: the collector passes the working-hours state, as pay has since 1.19.0), so a working-hours change inside the pay run moves the overlap flag with it.

**Who may see pay** (1.13.0, owner decisions round 4, Q1; narrowed 1.32.0, owner decisions 5 Nov; `canViewPersonPay(viewer, subject, roster, { context })`). A person's pay — day or hourly rate, pay basis, employment type (with its effective-dated transition), rate history, timesheets and invoices — is seen only by: the person (under any of their ids; their own rate read-only on My Profile); admins and super admins of the organisation (every person); a manager with the Operatives toggle, for an operative only; and the platform owner. **A line manager who is neither** sees a report's pay **only on the timesheets they review** — the rate, the overtime calculation and the invoice, so every timesheet looks the same — and nowhere else (no Operatives page, no profile rate, no rate history): the timesheet review screens pass `{ context: 'timesheetReview' }`; every other screen passes nothing (`context` `profile`). It is read live from the roster, so a level change recalculates it (no rights carry over). Before 1.32.0 every active line manager saw the rate everywhere. The read plans (`payProfileReadPlan`, `rateHistoryQueryPlan`) still load a report's pay for their line manager, and the security rules (`canViewPayOf`) still allow those reads, because the timesheet review prices from them; the narrowing is what each screen shows. **1.33.0 (owner decision 6 Nov, option C1 — accepted for launch):** this stays as it is. A line manager who is not an admin and has no Operatives toggle reads their reports' pay documents (`payProfiles`, rate history, the `operativeProfiles` mirror) **only for timesheet review**: the read plans ask for the viewer's own id and their reports' ids — never anyone else's, never a catalogue row — and every other screen shows them nobody's pay (`canViewPersonPay` with no context). The screens enforce that; the security rules allow the line manager's read of a report's pay documents, because they cannot tell which screen asked. A server-checked alternative (the rate given only for a sheet the line manager reviews) can follow after launch if the owner wants it. Every screen showing pay (the people profile, Manage Users, timesheets, User Timesheets, invoices, the weekly report's pay cells) asks `canViewPersonPay` first — from 1.13.0 the people profile on web; the others with the release that reads pay per person (step E of the move). Security rules cannot hide fields, and every member reads the roster's `users` documents, so pay moves to a private document per person per organisation: `organizations/{orgId}/payProfiles/{subjectId}` (`payProfilePath`; `subjectId` = the person's users id, or an `operatives` row id for a catalogue person with no account), fields `PAY_PROFILE_FIELDS` (`payBasis`, `dayRate`, `hourlyRate`, `employmentType`, `employmentTypeTransitionFrom`, `employmentTypeEffectiveAt`) plus `userId` and write metadata, read with `parsePayProfileDocument` and laid over the person with `overlayPayProfile` (the pay profile wins once it exists). A pay save writes the changed pay fields to the pay profile in the same batch as the profile (`payProfileWriteFieldsFromProfileWrite`), by whoever `canChangePersonPay` allows. **The move** (Deploy notes 1.13.0): apps write pay to both places and read the pay profile first; `scripts/backfill-pay-profiles.mjs` copies existing pay (`payProfileBackfillWrites`); a later release reads pay only per person; the platform owner then sets `platformConfig/app.privatePay` true (the rules then limit rate history, the `operativeProfiles` mirror and timesheets to pay viewers and refuse any pay value written back onto `users`, `operatives` or `managers`); the backfill's `--strip` step then removes pay from those documents. Only after the strip can no colleague read another's rate. **1.26.0, reading and writing with the switch:** while `privatePay` is on for the organisation (`securitySwitchOn`), a save writes pay only to the pay profile and the rate history — the `users` / `operatives` / `managers` write leaves every `PAY_PROFILE_FIELDS` key out (`sharedDocumentPayWrite`; Add User writes the invited pay to the placeholder's pay profile, and accepting the invitation writes the account without pay); rate history is read whole by admins and otherwise with one `userId ==` query per person whose pay the viewer may see (`rateHistoryQueryPlan`); and pay profiles are read whole by admins and otherwise by id (`payProfileReadPlan`), each laid over the roster person under their id, else a former invite id (`overlayPayProfile`). **1.26.1, one starting value for display and editors** (`payEditorStartingValues(person, profile, { privatePay })`): no single-person pay display or pay editor starting value reads `payProfiles` while `privatePay` is off for the organisation — it is the person's `users` / `operatives` document exactly as stored, because an older build may have left the pay profile stale. While it is on, the display (the people profile) and every pay editor (Manage Users / Edit User, the catalogue operative form, the rate dialogs) start from the same value, the person with their pay profile laid over (`overlayPayProfile`; own id, else a former invite id; the person's document when none is readable), and that same record is the baseline of the save: the changed-fields diff, the "rate changed?" test and the rate history's previous rate (`payRateChangeWrites` `previousProfile`). An editor never starts from, or saves back, an old rate left on a shared document. **1.27.0, catalogue operatives** (owner decision 7): a catalogue operative is an `operatives` row with no app account — no stored `linkedUserId` / `userId` / `uid`, a row id that is no roster member's id and no roster member on its email (`catalogueOperativePaySubjects(operatives, roster)` → `{ id: <row id>, email, catalogueOperative: true }`; the security rules: no users document of that id, no stored link and no `userEmails/{email}` claim of the organisation on its email). Its pay is seen by admins, the person on its email and managers with the Operatives toggle (`canViewPersonPay` with `catalogueOperative: true`; it has no line manager). Both apps pass the catalogue operatives with the roster to `payProfileReadPlan` (so an operatives manager reads `payProfiles/{rowId}` by id and the row is laid over with it, `overlayPayProfile`) and to `rateHistoryQueryPlan`, whose `people` plan now also lists `operativeIds` — one query `operativeId ==` <row id> `and userId == ''` each (`payRateHistoryWriteFields` stores an empty `userId` for a catalogue row; rows saved with no `userId` field at all are read by admins only). An `operatives` row that belongs to an account is that person's and is read through their users id. **1.27.0, the invited rate on accept** (owner decision 6): while `privatePay` is on, accepting an invitation copies the pay fields of the placeholder's pay profile, exactly, to the account's own `payProfiles/{authUid}` (`invitePayProfileCopyWrite`; nothing when the switch is off, there is no placeholder, the account already has a pay profile, or the placeholder's profile holds no pay field). It is written after `users/{authUid}` and before the email claim moves to the account; the security rules allow this one create only then (§16). With the switch off nothing changes. Known limit while it is on: an invitation accepted by an existing account of another company keeps the rate on the placeholder's pay profile (the placeholder stays; admins see it).

**Who may change pay** (`canChangePersonPay`). A person never changes their own pay, line manager or employment type on any path — `users/{uid}`, `operativeProfiles/{uid}`, `operativeDayRateHistory`, or the pay fields of `operatives/{id}` and `managers/{id}`. An admin may (including their own rate). A manager with Operatives access may change an operative's pay in the same company, never their own or another manager's; a manager's pay is changed by admins only. Resending an unchanged value is not a change.

**A self-employed person must have a pay rate** (1.14.0, owner decision). Self-employed people are paid from their rate, so a save that leaves a self-employed person (`normalizeEmploymentType`; a missing type reads as self-employed) without a rate is refused: `payRateRequiredError({ employmentType, payBasis, dayRate, hourlyRate })` returns `PAY_RATE_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` ("Set a day rate or hourly rate — self-employed people are paid from it.") when the rate for the chosen basis (`hourly` → `hourlyRate`, `day` → `dayRate`, no basis → either) is missing, blank, not a number, 0 or below. PAYE never needs a rate. Pass the person as they will be saved — a change to self-employed, immediate or effective-dated, is checked with the new type. Add User refuses the invite and Edit User refuses the save on every platform, with the message on the rate field, whenever the writer may set that person's pay (`canChangePersonPay`). People saved before this rule are listed for admins by `usersMissingPayRate(roster)` (active, not deleted, once per person by `personIdSet`).

**Timesheet review** (`timesheetEntryReviews`). Only the person's line manager or an admin writes `payrollLineReviews` and `extraEntryReviews`. The decision for a price-work or expense entry is `extraEntryReviews[entryId] { decision, revisedAmount, reviewedAmount, reviewedByUserId, reviewedAtMs }` (`timesheetEntryDecision`, written with `timesheetEntryReviewWriteFields` as dotted partial updates); a review stored under the entry id in another letter case is found too. Entries are read with `parseTimesheetEntries(sheetDocId, sheet, { timeZone })`: every entry with a title is kept whatever its id, a stored id keeps its stored case (UUID or not), and an entry stored without an id gets `timesheetEntryId(sheetDocId, kind, index, entry)` — a UUID-shaped id derived from the sheet id, the array, the position and the content, the same on every read and every platform, never random — and the next save writes it onto the entry. A review made against a different amount is stale and the entry is pending again; an entry nobody reviewed counts at its own amount. The inline `managerDecision` / `managerRevisedAmount` on an entry is still written for older builds and counts only when it lowers what is paid, or on a legacy sheet (no map) the line manager already signed. Owner saves go through `timesheetOwnerWriteFields`: it always removes the review maps and the manager sign-off and export fields (`TIMESHEET_MANAGER_SIGN_OFF_FIELDS`); the person writes `weeklyReportOverride` only when they have no line manager (self-signed), cannot change price work or expenses once the line manager has signed, and writes `managerNote` (their own note to the manager). The owner then sends only the fields that changed, as a partial update — never a full merge-set of the sheet. Every timesheet save, owner or reviewer, writes `timesheetChangedFields(base, next)` (1.9.0): the fields that differ from the sheet **as this editor loaded it**, never every difference from the server copy, so a signature, expense or price-work entry saved on the other platform after the load is kept; and whether the line manager has signed is read from the stored sheet, never a local copy. Whether a writer is a reviewer is `timesheetWriterIsReviewer`. **Nobody reviews their own timesheet** (1.30.2): the person whose sheet it is — under any of their ids (`personIdSet`: their id, `formerUserIds`, `sameEmailUserIds`, roster rows on the same email) — is never its reviewer, admins and the Super Admin included, and `userTimesheetSubjects` never lists the viewer (User Timesheets, its counts and the review page). Their own sheet is the owner flow (My Timesheets): a self-signed owner (`timesheetSelfSigned`) completes it with their own signature through `timesheetOwnerWriteFields` with `subjectHasLineManager: false` (writing `weeklyReportOverride`); anyone else, an admin with Timesheet self-sign off included, waits for their line manager (or, with none, the other admins). **The export notice** (1.32.0, owner decision 4 Nov): because an export never includes the exporter's own sheet, User Timesheets → Signed off shows `timesheetExportOwnSheetNotice({ ownSheetSigned, ownSheetExported })` — `TIMESHEET_EXPORT_OWN_SHEET_NOTICE` ("Your own timesheet is not in this export — nobody exports their own sheet. Your line manager or another admin exports it.") — when the viewer's own sheet for that period is signed and not exported.

**Price work job number** (1.30.1, owner decision 4 Nov; §26 has the shared rule). A price work entry (`priceWorkEntries[]`: `id`, `title`, `details`, `jobNumber`, `agreedManagerName`, `startDate`, `endDate`, `amount`) added on My Timesheets needs a job number (`priceWorkJobNumberError`, 1–60 characters, `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE`). An owner save is checked with `timesheetPriceWorkJobNumberError(previous, next)` against the sheet as the editor loaded it (else as stored): every entry the owner added, or whose own fields (`title`, `details`, `jobNumber`, `agreedManagerName`, dates, `amount`) they changed, needs one, so an edit of an older entry without a job number asks for it before it saves; an entry they did not touch — matched by id without case, or by content for one stored without an id — is never checked, so older entries never block a signature or a save, and a line manager's review (decision or revised amount) is not an edit and never asks. Entries are never rewritten or rejected on read. The sheet, its sign and review lists, the invoice (`timesheetInvoiceRows` rows carry `jobNumber`, 1.30.1), the weekly report price work rows and exports show the job number, "—" when an older entry has none (`priceWorkJobNumberText`). **There is no security rule for it on timesheet documents**: older iOS builds save price work entries without `jobNumber`, and a rule would refuse their whole timesheet save (signatures included), so the check is the rulebook and the apps only. Expense entries keep their optional job number.

**Pay arithmetic** (1.10.0) is `payForHours({ payBasis, dayRate, hourlyRate, paidHours, standardDayHours, otMultiplier })` — hourly: rate × hours × multiplier; day: day rate × (hours ÷ the organisation's paid day, `orgDayHours`) × multiplier — rounded with `roundPennies`; no app keeps its own. 1.12.0: a day rate with a paid day below 1 hour (`PAY_STANDARD_DAY_MIN_HOURS`), missing or not a number pays 0 (rate not set), never the day rate divided by a 0.01 floor; a multiplier that is not a positive number is 1; a rate that is not a number is 0.

**The unpaid break** (1.12.0, `payrollHoursResult`). On a weekday the break taken off a booking is the part of the break window (`breakWindowStart`–`breakWindowEnd`) the booking's clock time covers, up to `unpaidBreakMinutes`: a booking across the whole window loses the whole break; 11:50–12:20 against a 12:00–12:30 break loses 20 minutes. Nothing comes off when the break is paid (`breakPaid`), removed on the booking (`isBreakRemoved`) or the slot is a named AM / PM. **A booking whose clock time lies entirely within the unpaid break pays 0 hours** (12:00–12:30, 12:05–12:25) — when a booking has clock times it never falls back to a full standard day; only a booking with no clock times at all is paid by its slot (FULL DAY, AM, PM, EVENING, OVERTIME). **Lunch warning** (1.13.0): before a custom-hours booking is saved, every app shows `customHoursBreakWarning(policy, dayKey, booking)` — "You are booking custom hours that cover the unpaid lunch break. Turn off Unpaid break to make sure they are paid for this time." — as a confirm step when the booking is custom hours, the organisation break is unpaid (`breakPaid` not true, an unpaid length above 0), the booking's Unpaid break toggle is on (`isBreakRemoved` not true) and its hours overlap the break window that day (1.19.0: on a Saturday or Sunday only when that day's break is unpaid, `unpaidBreakAppliesOnDay`). The pay rule above is unchanged.

**Weekday pay** (1.20.0, owner decisions). A FULL DAY pays the weekday paid day — the standard day less the covered part of the unpaid break (`payrollHoursResult`; owner example 7.25h). Custom weekday hours pay as before: the hours inside the standard day less the unpaid-break overlap, and the hours outside it at the weekday overtime multiplier (06:30–16:30 → 7.25h + 2h × 1.5). Under a policy saved from 1.20.0 (`paidHoursDerived`) custom hours are never snapped to a half day (11:00–14:30 pays 3.5h, not half the day); a policy saved earlier keeps the 20-minute half-day snap. A stored AM / PM booking pays half `weekdayPaidHours` as before. The day a day rate is divided by is `weekdayPaidHours` of the policy in effect that day (`payForHours`, `reportDayValue`).

**Weekend pay** (1.13.0, Q2). Weekend bookings are custom hours (§4.3) and are paid by `payrollHoursResult` on that day's rule: with a set window, the hours inside it count as its "counts as" hours (pro rata when only part of it is worked) and the hours outside it are at that day's outside multiplier; with every hour at one rate, each hour × that multiplier. 1.19.0: when that day's lunch break is unpaid, that day's own break (length and window, the weekday ones when not set) comes off as §4.3 says (set window: off the credited hours for the part inside the window, off the outside hours for the part outside; every hour: (clock − break) × N). Every day is priced under the policy in effect on it; **a signed timesheet is never re-priced** — its `weeklyReportOverride` lines (and the signed-sheet invoice, `timesheetInvoiceRows`) keep their signed hours and amounts whatever the working hours later become. Stored weekend rows keep being paid as before and are never rewritten: an AM / PM pays half the "counts as" hours; a FULL DAY with no clock times pays the set window at its "counts as" hours with nothing outside it (`weekendFullDayWindow`; before 1.13.0 the weekday standard day plus weekend overtime), and on an all-hours day the pre-1.13.0 calculation (the standard day × that day's multiplier, e.g. 07:30–16:00 at 2× = 17).

**Overnight pay** (1.13.0, Q5): a booking that crosses midnight is paid wholly at its start day's rules (`payrollHoursResult`): on a weekday the hours inside that day's standard window count once (less the unpaid break they cover) and every other hour, after midnight too, is at that day's weekday multiplier; on a Saturday or Sunday that day's weekend rule. A weekday night is paid at the weekday overtime multiplier for every hour outside the standard window (owner confirmed). Before 1.13.0 it was paid as plain hours at 1×.

**Who takes part in timesheets** is `timesheetParticipant(user, dayOrPeriod, timeZone)`: never an inactive person or an anonymised profile; given a period, `showsMyTimesheets`; given a day, self-employed that day (1.11.0: `timesheetsEnabled` and operative mode no longer bring a PAYE person in). It decides the Weekly Report timesheet feed (§7, per payment run), the `timesheet_submission_reminder` recipients (§13) and the User Timesheets rows.

**Invoices** (1.10.0). The rows of a generated invoice are `timesheetInvoiceRows({ sheet, payrollLines, extrasMode })` on every platform: a signed sheet's `weeklyReportOverride` first (its signed amounts; declined and zero rows, and PAYE / leave lines, left out); otherwise the live lines (`invoiceLines`) with the line manager's review once signed (`payrollLineReview(sheet, lineId)`, found under the line id in any letter case), then price work and expenses with their decision (`timesheetEntryDecision`) in `decided` mode or at their own amounts in `raw` mode. 1.30.1: each row carries its `jobNumber` (the stored entry's or override row's, trimmed; labour: the override row's, else the live line's; '' when none — price work shows "—", `priceWorkJobNumberText`). 1.11.0: given `timesheetDays`, rows dated on a day the person was PAYE are left out (the PAYE-extras path is removed).

**Timesheet totals** (1.22.0, owner rule: a summary never disagrees with the invoice). Every figure that sums a sheet — the User Timesheets and My Timesheets list rows (hours, overtime, price work, expenses), the detail screen (hours subtotal, overtime, price work, expenses, extras and the total), the sign sheet and the signed-sheet value — is `timesheetTotals(input)`, where `input` is exactly the input of `timesheetInvoiceRows` and the money is the sum of its rows, so the summary total is the invoice total for the same inputs by construction. `{ hours, standardHours, overtimeHours, labour, standardLabour, overtimeLabour, priceWork, expenses, extras, total, submittedPriceWork, submittedExpenses, submittedExtras, pendingEntries, source, invoiceRowCount }`, money rounded to pennies. In `decided` mode a declined entry counts 0, an edited one its revised amount, a stale review (made against another amount) is pending and counts the entry's own amount, an unreviewed entry its own amount (`timesheetEntryDecision`, `timesheetEntryEffectiveAmount`); a signed snapshot (`weeklyReportOverride`) wins; PAYE days and leave lines never count. List rows use `decided` (the export invoice). A detail screen uses `decided` once the line manager has signed or while the viewer is reviewing, else `raw` (the owner's draft at the amounts entered); a reviewer before signing passes `applyLabourReviews: true` (new on `timesheetInvoiceRows`; missing = once the line manager has signed, as before), so their screen shows the invoice their signature will produce. Hours come from the live payroll lines: each booking's standard line carries `bookingPaidHours` (`payrollHoursResult.totalPaidHours`) and `bookingOvertimeHours`; without them a line's `paidHours` (× `otMultiplier` on an overtime line). Before 1.22.0 list rows summed every entry at its own amount (a declined expense still counted) and both apps re-implemented the entry decision on the detail screen.

**Calculation.** Hours, overtime and pay come from bookings, the working-hours policy in effect that day and payment runs (`payrollHoursResult`, `workedHoursSplit`). **A booking's own overtime rate** (1.29.0, §25): `otMultiplierOverride` on a labour or manager site booking (1–3 in 0.25 steps; missing = the organisation's rate, so every existing booking prices exactly as before) replaces the day's multiplier for the booking's overtime part only — weekday hours outside the working day, a set-window weekend day's outside hours, every hour of a weekend paid at a multiplier only — never its standard hours, in `payrollHoursResult` and so on every screen that prices or labels a booking (timesheets, invoices, Daily Overview, the weekly report, booking breakdowns). A self-employed person's overtime lines are priced at it. A signed timesheet is never re-priced: a rate changed after signing does not alter the signed sheet or its invoice. Timesheets are grouped by payment run (`timesheetPeriodFromInvoicing`, `selectableTimesheetPaymentRunPeriods`); status Awaiting / Signed off / Exported is `timesheetListBucket`.

**Annual leave on timesheets.** Each approved leave day in the period appears as an information line "Annual leave (full day / AM / PM)" for the person and their line manager, with no edit control and no hours or pay (`timesheetLeaveLines`, `timesheetLeaveLabel`); 1.20.0: given the working day in effect on each day, the line carries its clock range from `leaveHalfDayWindows` (`timeRange`, e.g. "Annual leave (AM) · 06:30–10:30"). Leave never appears on a generated invoice.

**Admin and operative mode** (1.11.0). An admin or super admin has no operative mode: a stray `operativeMode` flag on an admin is ignored everywhere — people lists (`parseUserDocument` → `operativeMode`, `accountKind`), permissions (`effectivePermissions`), the approval rules (`isAdminOutsideOperativeMode`, `adminOutsideOperativeMode`) and the security rules (`callerOrgRole`, `hbCallerIsOperative`). Only operative-level users get operative mode, at setup. (Before 1.11.0 such an admin decided only as a line manager.)

**Line manager.** A person's line managers are `assignedManagerUserIds` (else the single `assignedManagerUserId`; `hasNoLineManager` means none) — `lineManagerIds(user, roster)`. Given the roster, only active members count, an old invite id the roster knows as someone's `formerUserIds` / `sameEmailUserIds` is that person, and the person themself never counts under any of their ids (`personIdSet`: id, `formerUserIds`, `sameEmailUserIds`, roster rows with the same email). `hasActiveLineManager` is whether anyone is left. Line managers receive that person's timesheets for signing, annual leave requests and other approval items (`userTimesheetSubjects`, `leaveApproverIds`). With no active line manager (legacy data only), the active admins and super admins outside operative mode receive them — never "any manager". Nobody approves their own items. Routing is read at the time — changing the line manager on any platform re-routes everything, nothing is stored on the request.
- **Every operative and every manager needs an active line manager**, whatever the annual-leave self-book permission and whether annual leave is switched on for them. **An admin needs one too unless their self-manage toggles are satisfied** (1.28.0, `selfManageToggles(user).satisfied`): Annual Leave Management (`annualLeaveSelfBook`) on **and** Timesheet self-sign (`timesheetSelfSign`) on — or, for a PAYE admin (no My Timesheets, `isPayeAccount`: stored `employmentType` `paye`), Annual Leave Management alone. **The Super Admin** of the organisation being worked in always counts as having both toggles on and never needs one. Who that is is judged per organisation, the same as the security rules (`callerIsSuperAdminOf`): its creator (`creatorUserId`), whether or not the users document has `isSuperAdmin`, or a users document of that organisation with `isSuperAdmin` — never a Super Admin of another company working here as an admin (`isOrganizationSuperAdmin(viewer, org)`). Apps pass the result as `organizationSuperAdmin` on the person (`isSuperAdminAccount` falls back to the stored flag only when it is missing). `lineManagerRequired(user)`; an inactive person is not checked. `lineManagerRequiredError(user, roster)` refuses the save when a required line manager is missing and says what fixes it: an operative or manager `LINE_MANAGER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` ("Set a line manager — this person's timesheets and annual leave need approval."); an admin `ADMIN_LINE_MANAGER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` ("Set a line manager, or turn on both Annual Leave Management and Timesheet self-sign — this person's timesheets and annual leave need approval."); a PAYE admin `PAYE_ADMIN_LINE_MANAGER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` ("Set a line manager, or turn on Annual Leave Management — this person's annual leave needs approval."). Manage Users on every platform refuses that save. People who fail it are listed for admins by `usersMissingLineManager(roster)` (Manage Users and Settings → Data health); until fixed, their timesheets and requests go to the other active admins (`leaveApproverIds`, `timesheetSubmittedRecipients`), so nothing is stranded — an organisation always has its Super Admin, who is always satisfied.
- **Self-manage toggles** (1.28.0, admins): **Annual Leave Management** (`annualLeaveSelfBook`; on = their own leave saves approved, "Submit"; off = they request it, §9) and **Timesheet self-sign** (`timesheetSelfSign`, label "Timesheet self-sign", help "Sign your own timesheet and generate the invoice without a line manager's signature."). The two are independent of each other and of the line manager: either may be on while the admin has a line manager (leave self-booked but the timesheet signed by the line manager, or the reverse). **Self-signed timesheets** (`timesheetSelfSigned(user)`): an admin with Timesheet self-sign on, or the Super Admin — line manager or not. Their timesheet has no "note to manager" and no "pending manager signature" state: their own signature completes the sheet (generate invoice is ready), and their owner save may write `weeklyReportOverride` (`timesheetOwnerWriteFields` with `subjectHasLineManager: !timesheetSelfSigned(...)`). With the toggle off their line manager signs (or, with none, the other admins). **Having no line manager no longer self-approves anyone** (before 1.28.0 it self-signed and self-booked every admin without one). Timesheet self-sign exists for admins only (`ADMIN_ONLY_PERMISSION_FIELDS`): it is shown and editable only on admin profiles (`ADMIN_EDITABLE_PERMISSION_FIELDS`), shown greyed out for a PAYE admin (`TIMESHEET_SELF_SIGN_PAYE_NOTE`), on and locked for the Super Admin (`SUPER_ADMIN_SELF_MANAGE_MESSAGE`), never shown and always read off for a manager or operative (`effectivePermissions`). Demoting an admin keeps its stored value underneath (like every toggle since 1.15.0, `accountTypePermissions`), so a promotion back restores it; after the demotion the person needs a line manager. **Per organisation** (1.30.2, `organizationSelfManageFlags`): both toggles are read for the organisation being worked in — the person's users document when its `organizationId` is that organisation, otherwise their membership there (`users/{uid}/orgMemberships/{orgId}`, `parseOrgMembershipDocument`, the same source as `organizationLevelFor`), otherwise off; the organisation's creator reads both on. Never the users document of another company: a person's toggles in one company never apply in another. Admins set both toggles in Manage Users; nobody changes their own (`permissionToggleRows({ viewerIsSubject })` → `OWN_SELF_MANAGE_MESSAGE`, enforced by security rules, §16). No notification is sent.
- **Line managers and overtime** (1.29.0, §25): being a person's line manager also lets one apply that person's approved overtime to their bookings filed under their users id (§16). Whoever may set the line manager — admins, and operatives managers for operatives — therefore also decides who gets those writes.
- **`hasNoLineManager`** is a line-manager field: it is written only by whoever may change the line manager (admins; operatives managers for operatives), never by the person, together with the line-manager ids in one partial update. It is only valid for an admin or super admin, and (1.28.0) for an admin only with their self-manage toggles satisfied (`lineManagerRequiredError`). "No line manager" is offered to every admin (`lineManagerOptions`); the save says which toggles to turn on.

## 9. Annual leave

One document per request at `organizations/{orgId}/holidayBookings/{id}`: a run of calendar-consecutive days with the same slot is one record from `startDate` to `endDate` (`holidayRequestRanges`, `holidayRequestWriteFields`), with `timeSlot` (`FULL DAY` / `AM` / `PM`, applying to every day) and `status` (`pending`, `approved`, `rejected`). Cancelling approved leave is a request (`cancellationRequestedAt`, `cancellationRequestedByUserId`) that an approver accepts (the record is removed) or refuses. Either way the requester is notified (`holidayDecisionRecipients`: `holiday_request_approved` when accepted, `holiday_request_declined` when refused, 1.9.0), as its own notice that never reuses the original decision's row. Older one-document-per-day records stay valid. **Reading a record** is `parseHolidayDocument(docId, data, timeZone)` on every platform (no app keeps its own parser): it accepts the keys either app has written (start `startDate`/`start_date`/`start`/`from`/`dateFrom`/`holidayStart`/`date`; end `endDate`/`end_date`/`end`/`to`/`dateTo`/`holidayEnd`, else the start day; person `userId`/`user_id`/`uid`/`userID`/`linkedUserId`/`employeeUserId` or nested `user.id`, and `operativeId`/`operative_id`/`operativeID`; status `status`/`holidayStatus`/`approvalStatus`; slot `timeSlot`/`slot`/`halfDay`; note `decisionNote`/`note`), uses the document id as the record id, keeps ids in their stored case (an `operativeId` that is not a UUID is kept), turns dates into organisation-zone day keys (a calendar-date string is that day) and swaps a reversed range. Status words map through `normalizeHolidayStatus` (`HOLIDAY_STATUS_SYNONYMS`: requested/awaiting/submitted → pending; accepted/confirmed → approved; declined/denied/cancelled → rejected); a missing or unknown status is approved. Warnings, Daily Overview, leave coverage and timesheets take the parsed day keys as they are (`labourHolidayInput`) and never recompute them from the stored dates. A decision writes `status`, `approvedByUserId`, `approvedAt` and an optional trimmed `decisionNote` (blank clears it) shown to the requester (`holidayDecisionWriteFields`, `parseHolidayDecisionNote`). Day counting (`leaveRecordDayKeys`, `annualLeaveBalance`): a single-day record counts its day; a multi-day record counts each day except weekends and bank holidays, each in its own leave year. Submitting a request notifies its approvers (`holiday_request_submitted`, `holidayRequestRecipients`), and the requester is notified of the decision (`holidayDecisionRecipients`); see §13. Approved leave affects Daily Overview, unbooked-labour warnings (`leaveCoverageRows`), timesheets (information lines) and My Schedule. Booking someone onto a day of their approved or pending leave asks first (`bookingLeaveConflicts`, §5, 1.16.0). Editing an existing record is a partial update of the changed fields.

**Leave halves** (1.20.0, owner decision). Annual leave keeps AM / PM / Full day = 0.5 / 0.5 / 1 day (`leaveRecordDayKeys`, `annualLeaveBalance` unchanged). The halves split the Monday–Friday working day at its exact middle, ignoring the break: `leaveHalfDayWindows(policy)` (06:30–14:30 → AM 06:30–10:30, PM 10:30–14:30; 10:00–18:00 → AM 10:00–14:00, PM 14:00–18:00; floored to the minute), read under the working hours in effect that day. They are used only for leave: the Daily Overview, Schedule and My Schedule leave labels (`leaveHalfDayLabel`, e.g. "AM 06:30–10:30"), `bookingLeaveConflicts` (purple rows), `leaveCoverageRows` and timesheet leave lines (`timeRange`). A stored AM / PM booking compared with leave is that leave half (`leaveComparableInterval`); custom hours are their clock times. **Leave cover:** on a half-leave day the other half must be booked minute for minute, except that time inside the lunch-break window never needs covering (owner rule, as for unbooked labour, §5; the break does not move where the halves split): AM leave on 06:30–14:30 with lunch 10:00–10:45 plus a booking 10:30–14:30 → covered; plus 10:45–14:30 → covered; plus 11:00–14:30 → "10:45–11:00 (0.25 hours) is not booked"; on 10:00–18:00 (paid break 13:00–13:30) AM leave plus 14:00–18:00 → covered. Bookings keep `halfDayWindows`; no app splits a leave day itself.

**Self-booking** (`canSelfBookAnnualLeave(user, roster)`, 1.28.0): a person's own leave saves approved when they have the `annualLeaveSelfBook` permission (Annual Leave Management; 1.30.2: in the organisation being worked in, `organizationSelfManageFlags`) outside operative mode, or they are the Super Admin. Having no line manager never makes anyone a self-booker, admins included (before 1.28.0 an admin without one self-booked). Everyone else sends a pending request to `leaveApproverIds`. The submit button reads "Submit" when the leave saves approved and "Submit request" otherwise (`leaveSubmitLabel`, `LEAVE_SUBMIT_LABEL`, `LEAVE_SUBMIT_REQUEST_LABEL`). Self-booked leave notifies nobody. **Never delete leave when the toggle changes** (owner decision 4 Nov): switching Annual Leave Management on or off for a person only decides whether their *future* leave saves straight away or is sent as a request; their existing approved leave and pending requests stay exactly as they are, on every platform.

**Who decides** (`canDecideLeave(viewer, requester, roster)`): never the requester under any of their ids, admins included. An admin or super admin not in operative mode may decide any request in the organisation; anyone else only when they are in `leaveApproverIds(requester)` (an admin in operative mode decides only as a line manager). The list a person sees to decide — pending requests and cancellation requests — is `leaveDecisionQueue(viewer, requests, roster)`. Enforced by security rules.

**The leave calendar** (1.23.0, audit finding 19). What a person's annual-leave calendar shows on a day is `leaveDayKind(records, dayKey, timeZone, nonWorkingDayKeys)` → `{ kind, recordId, isLocked }`: a record covers the day only when the day is one of its leave days (`leaveRecordDayKeys` — a multi-day record skips weekends and the bank holidays given, the days the allowance counts); rejected records never show; approved leave with a pending cancellation request still shows as approved (as everywhere in §9); and the order is fixed, never the list order — a full day beats a half day, and at the same length pending beats approved (pendingFull, approvedFull, pendingHalf, approvedHalf, none). A full day is locked (`isLocked`): no more leave is requested on it. Before 1.23.0 web read the whole start–end range on the browser day and iOS hid leave with a pending cancellation.

**Holiday Report** (1.22.0) — one person's leave in one leave year — is `leaveReportRows({ records, personIds, onDayKey, startMonth, timeZone, nonWorkingDayKeys, statuses })` → `{ startDayKey, endDayKey, yearKey, rows: [{ id, startDayKey, endDayKey, timeSlot, status, days }], totalDays }` on every platform, with the year picker from `leaveReportYears({ firstDayKey, todayDayKey, startMonth })` and one record's days from `leaveReportDayCount(record, range, timeZone, nonWorkingDayKeys)` (`leaveDaysInRange`). The year is the organisation leave year (`leaveYearBounds` from `annualLeaveDefaults.startMonth`), never the calendar or device year; a multi-day record counts its working days only (weekends and the region's bank holidays out, `leaveRecordDayKeys`), each inside the year; AM / PM count 0.5; records are `parseHolidayDocument` rows matched under every id the person goes by (`personIdSet` plus their operative ids) without case; approved only unless `statuses` says otherwise. The report's total equals the `taken` figure of `annualLeaveBalance` for the same year. Before 1.22.0 iOS counted every calendar day from start to end, a half day as 1, the device's January–December year and exact ids only. Web has no Holiday Report screen.

## 10. My Schedule

Every person sees their own bookings and annual leave (approved, and pending marked as pending), matched by account (`bookingIdsForAccount`, with the roster's same-email ids as `aliasIds`; leave with `leaveRecordsForAccount`, 1.9.0, which from 1.10.0 also matches leave whose `userId` is the account's email, as older iOS builds stored it) — never by name. Every My Schedule screen shows it — on web both the read-only screen and the self-booking screen admins and managers get (before 1.12.2 the self-booking screen showed no leave, while Daily Overview did). It updates live when a booking or leave changes on any platform. Hours as in §6 (1.20.2: each row shows its times and `bookingHoursLabel`, the pay rule's hours for that day, on every list — My Schedule, Schedule, the project / small works week, Daily Overview).

## 11. Manage Users

All Manage Users changes — role, line manager, employment status and rates with effective dates, inactive flag, exclusions, invites — are online-only, sync to every platform through live listeners, and are written as partial updates of the changed fields only (`userProfileWriteFields`; a linked `operatives` / `managers` row with `operativeRowWriteFields` / `managerRowWriteFields`). Changing a person's pay from an operative or manager record writes only the pay fields of their `users/{uid}`, never the whole document. People lists use `rosterPeople` / `listedRosterUsers`; Active / Inactive / Pending is `userRosterSegment`. **Which list** (1.23.0, audit finding 9): `rosterListFor(kind, roster, claimedEmails)` with `kind` `operatives` | `managers` | `admins` — each person is on exactly one list, their level (`rosterListKind` = `permissionLevelOf`): admins (super admin, `adminAccess`, the admin role) on Admins only, never Managers; the operative level (operative mode, or the operative role) on Operatives; everyone else on Managers — the manager flag, the manager role, and a person with no level flag at all, who already reads as manager level for their toggles, the line-manager picker and Change user type. A `managers` row never moves a person between lists and never sets `manager: true` on a person (1.23.1, owner decision: web's roster no longer does; iOS never did). One document per email (`rosterPeople`), sorted by name. **Every user has a level** (1.23.1, owner decision): every create path writes one — Add User and re-invite (refused without one), accepting an invitation (the invitation's stored level), guided setup and sign-up (admin). A person with none (`userMissingLevel`: no `isSuperAdmin`, `adminAccess`, `manager` or `operativeMode` stored true and not an admin by role) is a data fault: Settings → Data health lists them (`usersMissingLevel`) with `USER_MISSING_LEVEL_MESSAGE` ("Has no user level: open in Manage Users and pick one") and a link to each person, where Change user type sets the level. Until then they read as manager level, are listed on Managers, and their clashes stay operative (High) clashes (owner decisions 1A, 2A). Before 1.23.0 web listed a person with no flag on Operatives and admins by role on Managers; iOS listed a person with no flag nowhere unless a `managers` row placed them on Managers. A save that leaves an operative or manager with no active line manager is refused with "Set a line manager — this person's timesheets and annual leave need approval." (`lineManagerRequiredError`, §8) — the self-book permission does not waive it. **1.28.0:** an admin may have none only with their self-manage toggles satisfied (Annual Leave Management and Timesheet self-sign on; PAYE: Annual Leave Management); otherwise the save is refused with the admin message, which says to set a line manager or turn on both toggles. The Super Admin never needs one. Admins see the people already in that state (`usersMissingLineManager`, Manage Users and Settings → Data health). **Admin permission rows** (1.28.0, `permissionToggleRows`): an admin's rows are all on and locked except the two self-manage toggles, Annual Leave Management and Timesheet self-sign (`ADMIN_EDITABLE_PERMISSION_FIELDS`); the Super Admin's two are on and locked; a PAYE admin's Timesheet self-sign is greyed out; the viewer's own two are never editable; Add User starts both off (`addUserPermissionDefaults`). A self-employed person needs a day or hourly rate above 0 (`payRateRequiredError`, §8): Add User and Edit User refuse the save with "Set a day rate or hourly rate — self-employed people are paid from it." on the rate field, and admins see an amber list of self-employed people without a rate (`usersMissingPayRate`). The line manager, `hasNoLineManager`, the self-book flag and (1.28.0) Timesheet self-sign are written by admins (operatives managers for operatives, never Timesheet self-sign) only. The live roster is the `users` query on `organizationId` (§1). A user may not change their own rates, pay basis, employment type, active flag, line manager or permissions — enforced by security rules. **Roles & permissions** opens Manage Users directly.

**Permission toggles by user level** (1.11.0, owner decisions round 3). The toggles in effect are `effectivePermissions(user)`; every permission check on every platform and in the security rules reads them, never the stored flag. The level is `permissionLevelOf(user)` (admin for a super admin, `adminAccess` or the admin role; else operative for operative mode or the operative role; else manager).
- **Admins and super admins:** every `USER_PERMISSION_FIELDS` toggle reads on and is locked (`lockedPermissionFields`) — Admin Access and Operatives included — except **Annual Leave Management** (`annualLeaveSelfBook`), the only toggle an admin can change (`ADMIN_EDITABLE_PERMISSION_FIELDS`). `operativeMode` reads off and is not shown. The level is changed only with "Change user type". The annual leave allowance fields (`annualLeaveEnabled`, days per year, leave year, carry-over) are not permission toggles and stay editable.
- **Managers:** the stored toggles, editable as before, except **Materials** and **Site audit**, always on and locked (`MANAGER_LOCKED_ON_PERMISSION_FIELDS`; both apps already saved them on). **Wholesalers** stays an editable toggle: a manager can have it off. **Operatives** off: they still schedule live active operatives onto the jobs they manage (`canScheduleOperatives`, `canWriteBooking`) but cannot add or manage operatives (`canManageOperatives`). **Materials catalogue** (1.33.0, owner decision 4 Nov): adding, editing and deleting catalogue items and prices is **admins only** (`canManageMaterialCatalogue`: admins and super admins outside operative mode) on both apps; managers keep the Materials toggle (job material lists, quotes and orders, `canSendMaterialOrders`) and still pick from the catalogue when adding material to a job, but no longer open the catalogue page.
- **Operatives:** Materials and Site audit, editable.
- Existing user documents are never rewritten for this: locked values are derived when read (`parseUserDocument.permissions` is `effectivePermissions`), and `userProfileWriteFields` never writes a locked toggle (`keepStoredLockedPermissions`: top-level, dotted and `permissions` map entries keep their stored value), except when "Change user type" changes the level.
- **One set of rows** for Add User, its summary and Edit User on both apps: `permissionToggleRows({ level, user?, mode: 'add' | 'edit' | 'summary' })` → ordered `{ field, label, help, visible, editable, value }` (`PERMISSION_TOGGLE_CATALOGUE`). Admin: every row shown on and greyed out except Annual Leave Management. Manager: every row but Admin Access; Materials and Site audit on and greyed out. Operative: Materials and Site audit. Add User starts from `addUserPermissionDefaults(level)` (1.14.0, owner decision): **every toggle the adder can change starts off** and the adder turns them on one by one; locked toggles keep their locked value (admin and super admin: every toggle on and locked except Annual Leave Management, which starts off; manager: Materials and Site audit on and locked; operative: Materials and Site audit both start off). This applies to new users only — changing an existing person's user type keeps every saved toggle (`accountTypePermissions`, 1.15.0) and never applies these defaults.

**Change user type** (1.11.0) is `accountTypeChangeWrites({ person, kind, organizationId, roster, permissions? })`. **A type change keeps every saved toggle** (1.15.0, owner decisions round 6): `accountTypePermissions(kind, previous)` returns the person's stored toggles (top level, else `permissions.{key}`; a toggle never stored keeps the value it reads as) and changes only the level flags (`adminAccess`, `operativeMode`, `manager`); the screen's `permissions` are taken only for the toggles the new level can change (`accountTypeWritePermissions`: an admin only Annual Leave Management). An admin still reads every toggle on and locked except Annual Leave Management (`effectivePermissions`); the stored values stay untouched underneath and read again after a demotion, so manager → admin → manager returns the exact toggles and operative → manager keeps the operative's. (Before 1.15.0 a change kept Projects, Small Works, Weekly Report and Daily Overview and preset the rest.) One batch writes every document that decides the type in this organisation — `users/{uid}` (role, level flags and every toggle, top level and `permissions.{key}`), same-email users documents of this organisation, `organizations/{orgId}.members.{uid}` and `users/{uid}/orgMemberships/{orgId}` (role and nested permissions). When the person's `users/{uid}.organizationId` is another company, that profile is not rewritten: only `members.{uid}` and the membership here are written (their level here is read from them, `organizationLevelFor`) and the screen shows `ACCOUNT_TYPE_OTHER_COMPANY_NOTE`. It is refused with `ACCOUNT_TYPE_SUPER_ADMIN_MESSAGE` for a super admin who would stop being an admin. The security rules allow these writes to an admin or super admin of the organisation only. The plan is written as **one atomic batch** (1.12.2) — never the users document first and the rest after — so a refusal leaves nothing half applied; an admin may read the person's membership (`users/{uid}/orgMemberships/{orgId}` get) to build it. A type that is not the same on the users document of this organisation and `members.{uid}` (or the membership) is **half applied** (`accountTypeSyncState`: an older iOS build wrote only `users/{uid}`, or a save stopped half way); the users document is the source, Manage Users lists those people and Edit User says `ACCOUNT_TYPE_OUT_OF_SYNC_MESSAGE`, and the next Save re-applies `accountTypeChangeWrites` with that type. One save runs at a time (a second press is ignored) and a saved profile returns to the people list. A profile save syncs names to same-email users documents of this organisation only (a users query on email alone is never allowed by the rules).

**Job managers** (1.30.3, owner bug 4 Nov). The manager picker on a project or small works — create and edit, on every platform — lists `jobManagerCandidates(roster)`: the active admins, super admins and managers, one per person (rows that are one person by `personIdSet` fold together and the active, signed-up users document speaks for them, as in `lineManagerCandidates`; level read from that row, shown as Admin / Manager, `JOB_MANAGER_LEVEL_LABELS`), never an operative, an inactive person, an invite not accepted yet (`passwordSet` false or status `pending`) or an anonymised deleted profile; with none, `JOB_MANAGER_NO_CANDIDATES_MESSAGE`. The roster passed is every users document of the organisation, **not** the email fold (`rosterPeople` keeps an admin document first, so a stale admin copy left by repeated Change user type saves stood in for a live manager and hid them on web, while iOS listed them). Each manager stored on a job (`jobManagerIds`) reads through `jobManagerAssignments(job, roster, catalogue)` → `{ storedId, state, candidate, name, reason, note }`: `current` (a candidate; matched by any of their ids without case or `user:` prefix, or a `managers` catalogue row — its id or `stableManagerId` — on their email), `former` (a known person who is no longer a candidate — `not_manager`, `inactive`, `pending`, `deleted` — or a catalogue row with no users document, `no_account`; the picker shows them with `JOB_MANAGER_FORMER_NOTES[reason]`, e.g. "Former manager — no longer a manager", and a remove action, never silently), or `unknown` (nothing loaded matches; kept so a save never drops an assignment). Job screens name only current managers as the job's managers. Change user type updates every users document of the organisation on the person's email (`accountTypeChangeWrites` with the unfolded roster), so the copies cannot disagree again.

**People and access** (1.31.0, access audit and owner decisions 4 Nov; `peopleAccess.ts`).
- **No rights carry over on a level change.** An operative is nobody's line manager: `lineManagerIds` (and so `leaveApproverIds`, `canDecideLeave`, `canViewPersonPay`, `canViewPayeClaims`, `userTimesheetSubjects`) skips a roster member at the operative level (`rosterMemberIsOperativeLevel`), even when an old id is still stored on the report; the security rules hold the same (`hbCanDecide`, timesheet review, `canViewPayOfUser`, `callerLineManagesUser` — overtime, price work and expense reads — and the claim-evidence storage rule).
- **A line manager is not made an operative, deactivated or deleted while anyone reports to them**: `lineManagerReports(person, roster)` (active people whose stored line manager ids name any of the person's ids, every users document of the organisation) and `lineManagerChangeBlock({ person, roster, change })` → `{ blocked, reports, message }` with `LINE_MANAGER_REPORTS_BLOCK_MESSAGE` ("This user is the line manager for the users below. Give each of them another line manager first, then try again."); the screen lists the reports and refuses the save. Admin ↔ manager never blocks.
- **Job managers on a level change**: admin ↔ manager keeps every job. Made an operative, deactivated or deleted (`changeRemovesJobManager`), the person is taken off every job they manage — `jobManagerRemovalWrites({ organizationId, person, change, jobs, roster, managers })`: every id of the person (`personIdSet`, the `user:` form, `managers` rows on their email by id and `stableManagerId`) out of `managerIds`, `managerId`, `managerUserIds` and `assignedManagerUserIds`; a list left empty is removed and `managerId` moves to the next manager or is removed. The job stays, with no manager when none is left, and the admins get `job_manager_removed` (company activity, `jobManagerRemovedNoticeText`). **A new project or small works needs a manager** (`jobManagerRequiredError`, `JOB_MANAGER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` "Please assign at least one manager"; the security rules refuse a create without one).
- **Every Manage Users write reaches every users document of the person** in this organisation (the unfolded roster, not the email fold): Change user type (`accountTypeChangeWrites` — the person's document gets only the fields whose stored value differs, so a type change never writes back a toggle another admin changed since; same-email documents get the level flags and role; a same-email document already listed in `members` gets the new role there too, never a new entry; leaving admin clears `hasNoLineManager`), deactivate / reactivate (`userActiveWrites`: `isActive` on each users document of this organisation, `accountActive` on the membership; 1.33.0: the person's own users document is skipped when it names another company — they are deactivated here through the membership — and the membership is only ever updated, never created: `membershipExists: false` leaves it out, and the apps update it rather than merge-set it, so no bare `{ accountActive }` membership is made), and the Super Admin transfer (`superAdminTransferWrites`). `rosterListFor` folds before it classifies (one list per person; a live admin document wins the fold, never a stale admin copy), so the copy a list shows is the one the type change wrote.
- **One flag reader**: `userPermissionFlag(user, key)` — the top-level value when stored, else `permissions.{key}` — for every toggle and self-manage rule (`effectivePermissions`, `selfManageToggles`, `canSelfBookAnnualLeave`); firestore.rules `staffFlag` reads the same way. Before 1.31.0 the self-manage rules and the security rules counted a flag on when it was on in either place.
- **Change Super Admin** is one batch, `superAdminTransferWrites({ organizationId, fromUserId, toUserId, previousCreatorUserId, roster })`: the organisation's `creatorUserId` and `members.{to}` admin; the new Super Admin `isSuperAdmin` with both self-manage toggles on (their card shows Annual Leave Management and Timesheet self-sign on and greyed out, `SUPER_ADMIN_SELF_MANAGE_MESSAGE`); every outgoing Super Admin (and the previous creator) `isSuperAdmin` false, admin — and, when they have no active line manager, both self-manage toggles written on, so they keep the self-approval they had (owner to confirm; the alternative was to refuse the transfer until they had a line manager). Their memberships here are written in the same batch.
- **A demoted admin keeps Annual Leave Management** as they had it (`accountTypePermissions`; a Super Admin's, never stored, reads on). **Wholesalers** stays visible and editable for managers (`permissionToggleRows`).
- **Line manager set once, then locked** (1.33.0, owner decision 6 Nov): `canChangeLineManager(actor, target, { isCreate, invitation })`. Admins and super admins change anyone's line manager at any time. A manager with the Operatives permission sets an operative's line manager only when adding them (`isCreate`: Add User, where a line manager is required, `lineManagerRequiredError`) or on an invitation they sent that is not accepted yet (`invitation` `{ accepted, invitedBy }`); after that only an admin changes it (deleting and re-adding the operative is the accepted way round). Managers without the toggle, operatives and the person never do. A save that changes it (`lineManagerChanged(before, after)`: the stored ids without case and order, or `hasNoLineManager`) by someone who may not is refused with `LINE_MANAGER_LOCKED_MESSAGE` ("Only an admin can change the line manager once this person has been added."); Edit User shows the picker read-only to them. `LINE_MANAGER_FIELDS` = `assignedManagerUserIds`, `assignedManagerUserId`, `hasNoLineManager`.
- **"No line manager" is for admins only**: the security rules refuse setting `hasNoLineManager` on anyone who is not an admin (resending a stored true passes); operatives managers never set it.
- **Operatives managers** (owner decision 7): a manager with the Operatives permission adds, edits, deactivates and deletes operatives — operative level only, with or without a login — never a manager, an admin, themselves, or their own pay. Delete is `canDeleteUser` / `userDeleteRefusal` with `actorManagesOperatives` (`operatives_only` otherwise). The security rules allow them: the operative's users document (names, contact, trade, active flag, Materials / Site audit, leave and pay — the person stays an operative; the line manager only as below, never a privilege or a self-manage toggle); the delete plan's documents (users document, tax ids, membership, email claim, the one `members` entry with value `operative`, the operatives row, and — once the users document is gone — the person's labour bookings **dated after today** in the organisation zone, each with its tombstone (`operativesManagerMayDeleteBooking`; owner decision: today's and past bookings the delete leaves as they are, as history; 1.33.0: there is no cancel, and the Operatives permission adds no booking power — only an admin or the job's manager deletes those, `canDeleteBooking`). Admins are unchanged (today on, `bookingsToRemoveOnOperativeDelete`). Because of the rules' document-lookup budget, their delete runs the account step as separate writes in the plan's order (tax ids, membership, email claim, then the users document, then the members entry); an admin's stays one batch. Memberships are admin writes, so their deactivate writes the users documents only. The admins get `operative_created` and `operative_deleted` (company activity).
- **Atomic saves**: Edit User writes the profile, its pay history and pay profile, the email claim, the same-email names, the linked `operatives` / `managers` row names and the `operativeProfiles` mirror in one batch; Add User writes the invitation, the placeholder and the email claim in one batch (the pay profile and tax ids follow, as their rules read the placeholder).
- **Exported timesheets** (owner decision; §8): the Exported list and its hub count show `exportedTimesheetHistorySubjects({ viewer, members })` → `{ userId, own }[]` — everyone on User Timesheets (`userTimesheetSubjects`, unchanged: nobody reviews their own sheet), **the viewer's own exported sheets** under any of their ids (`own: true`, read-only — `timesheetWriterIsReviewer` is unchanged), and **inactive or anonymised people in scope** (admins: every timesheet user; managers: the people they line-manage). Before 1.31.0 a reviewer's own exported sheets vanished from the list (a PAYE admin's showed nowhere) and inactive people's exported sheets never showed. A sheet stored without `userId` is matched by its document id (`timesheetDocumentSubjectId`), never dropped. The security rules' reads are unchanged (the person, their line manager and admins already read these sheets).
- **Unbooked labour on web** reads the roster fold's same-email ids (`userIdAliases`) as the person's (`labourPersonFromUser`), as iOS already did.
- **PAYE price work, expenses and their reports and evidence (§26)** follow the same people rules: a demoted manager stops reading a report's claims and evidence (`canViewPayeClaims` over `lineManagerIds`; rules `callerLineManagesUser` and the storage rule); `priceWorkApproverCandidates` never offers an operative (unchanged); claims match the person under every id (`personIdSet`); deactivating the person stops their approvals (inactive members never count); an operatives manager gains no claim rights; the price-work job number rule is unchanged.

**Line managers on Manage Users** (1.11.0): the picker lists `lineManagerCandidates(person, roster)` — the active admins, super admins and managers, never the person under any of their ids, never a pending invite or a deleted profile, by name; **one option per person** (1.14.0): roster rows that are the same person by `personIdSet` (two users documents on one email, an id in another letter case, a former invite id) are one option whose value is the person's live users document (active, signed up, not listed as another row's alias), and the level and status are read from that row; the selected values are `lineManagerSelectedIds(person, roster)` — a stored id that names the person's other document (or another letter case) shows that one option as selected; "No line manager" is offered only when `lineManagerOptions(person).allowsNoLineManager` (`lineManagerRequired` false: admins); the row reads `lineManagerSummary(person, roster)` → `{ label, ids: [{ id, name }] }` — each stored id resolved on the roster (an unknown id reads "Unknown person", never dropped), the label the first name with "+N more" only for N ≥ 1, "Not set" when none is stored, "No line manager" when chosen. **Changing the account type on Add User** (1.24.0): the picks become `lineManagerSelectionAfterLevelChange(picks, newLevel, roster, person?)` → `{ lineManagerIds, hasNoLineManager, dropped }` — each picked id that is still a `lineManagerCandidates` row is kept (as that candidate's id, once per person, in picked order) and the rest are dropped; "No line manager" is kept only when the new level allows it (admins, `lineManagerOptions`). Who may be picked does not depend on the level, so a type change keeps valid picks and drops "No line manager" when leaving admin. Picking the same type again changes nothing. The save is still checked by `lineManagerRequiredError`. **The invitee is never their own line manager** (1.27.0, owner decision 1): Add User passes the invite as it will be saved — `inviteDraftLineManagerSubject({ email, level })` → `{ id: INVITE_DRAFT_PERSON_ID ('invite-draft'), email, role, … }` — as `person` to `lineManagerCandidates`, `lineManagerSelectionAfterLevelChange` and `lineManagerRequiredError`, so the roster rows on the typed email are never offered or counted as the invitee's own line manager. Before 1.27.0 web passed no person (iOS already passed the draft).
