- **One list from every source** (1.23.0, audit finding 21): `mergeVariationSources(collectionRows, fallbackRows, itemDocs)` — the `variations` collection, the older iOS settings-log fallback and the one-document fallbacks, one row per id (without case); the newer `updatedAtMs` wins and, on a tie, the collection row, then the item document, then the settings log. The open-variations badge is `openVariationCount(rows)`: rows not deleted whose status is `open` (any case). The rule both apps already used, written once.

## 23. Home, tasks and deadlines (1.23.0)

Audit findings 12 and 18: each app counted these on its own day (web London or the browser, iOS the device) and by raw ids.
- **One day.** Every figure uses the organisation-zone day of now (`organizationTimeZone`); a stored due date is turned into that zone's day with `dueDayKey` (any stored shape `holidayDocumentDayKey` reads).
- **Home overview** — `homeOverviewMetrics({ nowMs, timeZone, viewer, roster, operatives, managers, tasks, bookings, managerSiteBookings, holidays, nonWorkingDayKeys, scope })` → `{ dayKey, tasksDueToday, tasksDueThisWeek, tasksOverdue, outstandingTasksAllUsers, operativesOnSiteToday, managersOnSiteToday, peopleOnSiteToday, operativesOnALToday, managersOnALToday }`. A task is the viewer's when any assignee (operative or manager ids) is one of the viewer's ids (`personIdSet` of their account and email over the users, `operatives` and `managers` rows); due this week is today and the next six days; open tasks counts every task given that is not completed. People on site are the people with a labour booking today that occupies the day, or a manager schedule booking today on a project or small works — each person once (`peopleOnSiteToday` is not the sum of the two when one person has both). People on leave are active people with approved leave covering today (`leaveRecordDayKeys`), on the Operatives list (or with only an `operatives` row) or on the Managers list (§11); admins are not counted. **1.33.0:** with `scope` (the viewer's `dailyOverviewScopeFor`, §6) the on-site and on-leave figures count only people in it — a manager's own team; admins everyone. The "active projects" figure stays `activeJobsCount` (§21).
- **Tasks** — `isTaskOverdue(task, nowMs, timeZone)`: not completed (`normalizeProjectTaskStatus`) and due before today; a task due today is not overdue.
- **Deadlines** — `deadlineUrgency(item, nowMs, timeZone)`: complete → overdue (before today, even when blocked) → blocked → today → tomorrow → soon (2–3 days) → this week (4–7 days) → later; `deadlineDaysRemaining` for the days. The rule both apps used, on the organisation day.

## 24. Uploaded files (1.25.0)

Launch checklist 2.2: an uploaded file is never deleted outright or lost by someone who did not upload it. `lib/canonical/storedFiles.ts`; `storage.rules` enforces the same rule on the server.
- **Where files live.** Every upload is under `organizations/{orgId}/…` (`storedFileKind(path)` → `{ kind, organizationId, objectName, ownerUserId }`, `STORED_FILE_KINDS`): profile photos `userProfiles/{userId}/profile.jpg`; the company logo `branding/company_logo/{name}`; timesheet exports `timesheetExports/{seconds}_{name}`; qualification certificates `operatives/{operativeId}/qualifications/{qualificationId}/certificates/{name}`; variation evidence `healthSafety/{jobId}/variations/{name}` (web), `healthSafety/{jobId}/variationEvidence/{name}` and `variations/{variationId}/{name}` (iOS); site audit photos `siteAudits/{auditId}/images/{name}`; task files `tasks/{taskId}/{name}` (web), `tasks/{taskId}/files|images/{name}` (iOS); health & safety files (talks, RAMS, other, deadlines) `healthSafety/{jobId}/{category}/{name}`; (1.30.0, §26) PAYE price work / expense evidence `payeClaims/{userId}/{name}` (`paye_claim_evidence`, the person's own folder: they upload; they, their line managers and admins read; deleted as any other file) and personal site audit photos `personalSiteAudits/{ownerUserId}/{auditId}/{name}` (`personal_site_audit`: the owner only reads, writes and deletes — `fileDeletePermission` gives admins `owner_only`). Any other prefix is refused for everyone.
- **Every upload is a new object.** Its name is `storedFileObjectName({ uid, unixMs, extension })` → `{uid}_{unixMs}.{ext}` (the uploader's Auth uid; the original file name is never copied in), so a replacement — a new certificate, a new RAMS, a new logo — never overwrites the file it replaces and every stored link keeps working. `storedFileUploader(path)` reads the uploader and upload time back from the name. Two exceptions keep their fixed names because both apps read them: the profile photo (`profile.jpg`, replaced by the person or an admin; the replaced version is kept by the bucket's soft delete) and timesheet exports (`{seconds}_{name}`, no uploader in the name).
- **Who may delete** — (1.27.0, owner decision 3: kept as it is — an admin or super admin outside operative mode may still delete any file of their organisation; a deleted object is recoverable for `FILE_RECOVERY_RETENTION_DAYS`.) `fileDeletePermission({ storagePath, organizationId, viewerUserId, viewerRole, nowMs, uploadedAtMs? })` → `{ allowed, reason, undoEndsAtMs }`: an admin or super admin of that organisation outside operative mode (`reason: 'admin'`); for a profile photo also the person it belongs to (`own_profile_photo`); company logos and timesheet exports admins only (`admin_only`); every other file also its uploader, within `FILE_UNDO_WINDOW_MINUTES` (15) of the upload (`uploader_undo`; after that `undo_expired`) — for a retried upload or a file that uploaded but failed to link. Nobody else, managers included (`not_uploader`); never a file in another organisation (`other_organization`). Uploader ids compare exactly (Auth uids are case-sensitive). The server uses the object's `timeCreated`; an app passes the stored upload time when it has one, else the time in the name. A file whose name does not follow the pattern (uploaded before October 2026) has no provable uploader: admins only.
- **Overwriting an existing object** is allowed only to its uploader inside the same window (a retry of the same path), and for a profile photo to the person or an admin. An admin deletes but never overwrites another person's file.
- **Removing a file from a record** (variation evidence, a health & safety row, a task attachment, a certificate) deletes the Storage object only when `fileDeletePermission` allows it; otherwise the record drops its link and the object is kept, and the app says `FILE_KEPT_ON_REMOVE_COPY` ("Removed from this list. The file itself is kept so an admin can restore it."). Deleting a job, task, audit or H&S row never deletes its files.
- **Removed variation evidence is recorded** (1.27.0, owner decision 4). Unlinking an evidence file from a variation is one partial update (`variationEvidenceRemovalWriteFields(stored, { evidenceId, removedBy, removedAt })` → `{ set: { evidence, evidenceCount, removedEvidence } }`, built from the stored document, on `variations/{id}` and its `settings/variationItem_{id}` copy): the entry leaves `evidence` and `{ path, name, removedBy, removedAt }` is appended to the variation's `removedEvidence` list (`path` the Storage path, else the download URL). Nothing is ever taken off that list and no other save writes it. (Known gap: the apps keep this list append-only and web removes evidence in a transaction; the security rules do not yet refuse a staff write that shortens it.) `parseVariationDocument` returns it as `removedEvidence` (oldest first). Admins and super admins outside operative mode (`canViewRemovedEvidence`) see it in the variation detail under `removedEvidenceHeading(n)` — "Removed evidence (n)" — with the name, path, who removed it and when, to find and restore the file.
- **Server-side deletes.** Only the company eraser (§20, `eraseDeletedOrganizations`, day 61 after a company deletion) deletes Storage objects, with the admin SDK: everything under `organizations/{orgId}/`.
- **Recovery** is the bucket's Google Cloud Storage soft delete (deleted and overwritten objects are kept for the retention period and can be restored), switched on and checked by the platform owner in the Cloud console (Deploy notes 1.25.0). No app or rule changes bucket settings. **Retention is 30 days** (1.27.0, owner decision 5; `FILE_RECOVERY_RETENTION_DAYS`): deleted and replaced files, and the files of a company the §20 eraser has erased, are kept for up to 30 days, then permanently removed. Both apps' privacy policy (Retention) and data processing agreement (Deletion and Return) say so in one sentence, word for word: `DELETED_DATA_RETENTION_NOTICE` ("A deleted company can be restored for 30 days, after which its data is erased. Deleted files, including the files of an erased company, are kept for up to 30 days so they can be recovered, then permanently removed.").

## 25. PAYE overtime (1.29.0)

Owner-approved spec of 4 Nov ("PAYE overtime, price work and expenses", release 1.29). `lib/canonical/payeOvertime.ts`; the payrun is §4.5 (`payePayrun.ts`). Hours only — no money anywhere for PAYE. Price work, expenses and personal site audits are 1.30.

- **Who sees what.** On Timesheets, a button **Overtime, Expenses & Price Work** (`OVERTIME_HUB_LABEL`) sits below My Timesheets and above User Timesheets (`overtimeHubAccess({ viewer, roster })` → `{ showButton, mine, users }`): **My Overtime, Expenses & Price Work** for a PAYE person (stored `employmentType` `paye`); **Users' Overtime, Expenses & Price Work** for an admin and for anyone who line-manages at least one active person — admins see every active person, a line manager their reports (`overtimeUserSubjects(viewer, roster)`), with a search bar and a filter; tapping a person opens Overtime, Expenses and Price work, read-only except for the approval actions. A self-employed operative who line-manages nobody sees no button. Expenses and Price work are §26 (1.30.0).
- **The Overtime screen** opens on **Current PAYE payrun**, **Past PAYE payruns** (newest first, `payePastPayruns`) and **Custom date range** (start and end, then View Overtime; reaches older records and can generate a report). Until a PAYE payday is set it says `PAYE_PAYDAY_NOT_SET_MESSAGE` and only Custom date range works.
- **What counts.** `payeOvertimeLines({ personIds, bookings, organization, timeZone, range, reviews, requests })`: each booking of the person (every id they go by, without case) in the range, priced by `payrollHoursResult` under the working hours in effect that day (`overtimeForBooking`): weekday hours outside the working day × the weekday multiplier; a weekend set window, the hours outside the window × that day's rate; a weekend paid at a multiplier only, every hour × the rate; an unpaid break stays plain hours (`payrollMultipliedHours`). Only bookings with at least 15 minutes of overtime clock time (`OVERTIME_MIN_MINUTES`) are lines, one per booking (two jobs on one day are two lines). A line's title is the job number and name; under it the day, start–finish and the working day ("day 07:30–16:00"); the figure is `multipliedHoursText` ("1h × 1.5 = 1.5h").
- **The booking's overtime rate** is the stored `otMultiplierOverride` (the field the booking editors and `payrollHoursResult` already used; §8): 1–3 in 0.25 steps (`OVERTIME_MULTIPLIER_STEPS`, `overtimeMultiplierValid`, `OVERTIME_MULTIPLIER_MESSAGE`); missing = the organisation's rate. A stored rate is never clamped or re-priced on read. The cog and Edit Booking edit the same field, so changes go back and forth freely; editing times in scheduling keeps it.
- **States** (`OVERTIME_LINE_STATES`, labels `OVERTIME_LINE_STATE_LABELS`): **Needs review**; **Waiting for line manager** (a pending request names the booking and it still matches); **Approved** (a review whose fingerprint is the booking's current one); **Booking changed since request** (a pending request names it but the booking changed — it cannot be approved until sent again); **Booking cancelled** (cancelled by an approved red X, shown struck through). The fingerprint is `overtimeBookingFingerprint(booking)` — day, slot, start, finish, rate, the No-unpaid-break toggle and status — so any change to the booking after a decision puts the line back to Needs review (`overtimeLineIsStale`).
- **The cog** changes start, finish and rate with live hours: `overtimeCogPreview` → the old figure struck through beside the new one (on its own line on phones). **The tick** approves the line as it is. **The red X** ("I didn't work these overtime hours") is `overtimeStrikeOutcome`: a weekday booking partly outside the working day is trimmed back inside it (06:30–14:00 on 07:30–16:00 → 07:30–14:00); a weekend set-window booking is trimmed to the window; a booking with no hours inside the day or window, or on a weekend paid at a multiplier only, is **cancelled** (status Cancelled, never deleted, so a manager can restore it) after `OVERTIME_CANCEL_WARNING` — "Remove this booking?", the day · job · times, "This will remove this booking entirely. Your organisation pays a set multiplier for this day. If you still worked this day, use the cog to set the hours you worked and agreed.", Go back / Remove booking, "A manager can restore it if this was a mistake." A manager site booking is trimmed the same way but never cancelled (it has no Cancelled status; `OVERTIME_CANCEL_NOT_ALLOWED_MESSAGE` says to use the cog). Every booking change is `overtimeBookingWriteFields` → `{ set, remove }` of `workStartTime`, `workEndTime`, `timeSlot` (a Full day becomes `CUSTOM_HOURS` when its times change), `otMultiplierOverride` and `status` only — a partial update.
- **Who changes what** (`canEditOwnBookingOvertime`). **1.32.0 (owner decisions 4 Nov), the app passes the person and the roster:** the Super Admin directly; anyone with an active line manager (`hasActiveLineManager`) as a **request** to that line manager — admins included; anyone with none directly where the security rules let them write the booking (an admin, or a manager `canWriteBooking` allows), otherwise as a request. (Before 1.32.0: a PAYE admin directly; a PAYE manager directly where `canWriteBooking` lets them edit the booking, otherwise as a request; a PAYE operative always as a request.) **Which days** (1.32.0, owner option 2): ✓ ✕ ⚙ work on every day up to the end of the current PAYE payrun, future days of it included — `overtimeEditableUntil(organization, todayKey)` (today when no PAYE payday is set) passed as `editableUntilKey`; later payruns stay locked. Online only. A direct change writes the booking and the line's review (`overtimeDirectWrites`); when the booking changes, `booking_hours_changed_by_owner` goes to the job's managers and the person's line managers (§13).
- **Buttons.** A PAYE operative: **Send for approval** (always enabled; sends every line in the range, changed or not) and **Generate overtime report** (enabled when every line is approved and unchanged since — `canGenerateOvertimeReport`). A PAYE manager or admin: Generate overtime report, enabled once every line has a tick, X or cog (each of which writes its review).
- **Requests** (online only). `overtimeRequestLineFields` per line (keep, edit with the proposed start / finish / rate, or strike — trim, or `cancel`), each with the booking's fingerprint and the hours before and after; `overtimeRequestWriteFields` → `overtimeRequests/{id}` `{ userId, rangeStart, rangeEnd, status: 'pending', lines }`. Sending again replaces the person's pending request for the same range (`overtimeRequestsToReplace`, withdrawn in the same batch); the person may withdraw a pending request (`overtimeRequestWithdrawWriteFields`). The approvers — the person's active line managers and every active admin — each get the push (`overtimeRequestRecipients`), and any of them may decide (`canDecideOvertimeRequest`; never the requester). **A request only ever names and changes the requester's own bookings**: `overtimeRequestLineFields` / `overtimeRequestWriteFields` (with `personIds` and `bookings`) leave out any booking on someone else, and the approval re-checks every live booking against the requester's ids (`overtimeBookingIsTheirs`) and leaves out — and names — any that is not theirs (`foreignBookingIds`). In Users' → that person → Overtime they tick, cross or counter-change (cog) each line; **1.32.0 (owner decisions 4 Nov): one Approve button** (`OVERTIME_APPROVE_LABEL`) under the lines, enabled once at least one line is decided (`overtimeRequestAnyDecided`), approves **what the decider may apply and leaves the rest waiting** — see *Partial approval* below. Before 1.32.0 **Approve Overtime Request** was enabled only when every line was decided (`overtimeRequestFullyDecided`) and ran `overtimeApprovalWrites` in **one transaction** that re-reads every booking: any booking that no longer matches its line's fingerprint refuses the whole approval (`OVERTIME_REQUEST_STALE_MESSAGE`, nothing applied — an old request never overwrites a newer booking); a booking deleted meanwhile is dropped and named, and one the decider cannot read is left out and named (`unreadableBookingIds`, shown as "Can't read this booking"); a line whose booking change the decider may not write — a line manager on a booking filed under an `operatives` row, on a job they do not manage (`overtimeDeciderMayWriteBooking`: an admin, a `canWriteBooking` writer, or the line manager of a booking whose person id is the requester's users id) — refuses the approval with `OVERTIME_NEEDS_ADMIN_MESSAGE` (an admin or the job's manager approves it); the screen runs the same check first, so it never offers an approval the rules would refuse; every other line applies its decision (tick: the request's proposal; X: the strike outcome; cog: the decider's times and rate) as booking writes plus one review per line with the booking's new fingerprint, and the request becomes approved (`decidedBy`, `decidedAt`, `decisions`, `appliedBookingIds`, `droppedBookingIds`). The person gets "Overtime update" (`overtimeReviewedRecipients`). **Partial approval** (1.32.0): `overtimeApprovalWrites` still runs in one transaction that re-reads every booking, but no longer refuses the whole approval. Each decided line the decider may apply is applied (booking write and review); every other line is named in `lineOutcomes` and stays waiting on the request or is left out: `undecided`, `stale` (changed since the request — never overwritten; "Changed since the request — ask them to send it again."), `needsAdmin` (owner decision A: a line whose booking is filed under an `operatives` row shows **"Needs an admin"** to a line manager who does not manage its job — whatever the decision, a tick on a keep line or a cog that changes nothing included (review fix r274), `OVERTIME_NEEDS_ADMIN_LABEL`, `overtimeLineOutcomeLabel`), `refused` (an X that is not allowed), and the left-out `deleted` / `unreadable` / `foreign`. Lines applied by an earlier partial approval are skipped (`alreadyApplied`); a request line in `appliedBookingIds` or `droppedBookingIds` no longer waits (`payeOvertimeLines`). The request stays `pending` with `decisions`, `appliedBookingIds` and `droppedBookingIds` accumulated until nothing is left waiting, then becomes `approved` (the rules allow the decider to write `status` `pending` or `approved`, §16). The message is `reviewApprovalMessage`: "Approved 2 of 5. 2 need an admin; 1 not decided yet.", or with nothing applied "Nothing was approved: …" (`nothingApproved`, nothing written). **All records for this person** (1.32.0, platform guardrail 1): loading, sending and approving use one id list, `overtimePersonIds({ person, roster, operatives })` — `personIdSet` plus every `operatives` row on the person's email (`bookingIdsForAccount`), never one row picked by email. The line manager's decision is final; the person sees old → new; disagreements are sorted out off the app and a scheduler fixes the booking, which the overtime list follows.
- **Reviews** `overtimeReviews/{userId}_{bookingId}` (`overtimeReviewDocId`): `{ userId, bookingId, collection, decision: approved | edited | trimmed | cancelled, fingerprint, reviewedBy, reviewedAt, requestId }`, read with `parseOvertimeReviewDocument`. Old bookings have no reviews, so in a custom range they show as Needs review; nothing old is rewritten or re-priced.
- **Reports.** Generate overtime report saves a copy (`overtimeReportSnapshot` → `overtimeReports/{id}` `{ userId, userName, companyName, rangeStart, rangeEnd, kind: 'overtime', lines, totals, generatedBy, generatedAt }`, written once and never changed, `parseOvertimeReportDocument`) and builds the PDF from it (`overtimeReportRows`): company name and logo, the person, the range, one line per day and job with the final figures (start–finish, overtime hours × rate = total), and the total; hours only. Several reports per payrun are allowed; Past PAYE payruns lists them. The PDF is shared by the person (web download, iOS share sheet); no in-app approval of the report. If a booking changes after a report, the live figures and the weekly report follow, the line needs approval again, and the saved report stays exactly as it was.
- **The rate when booking** (1.32.0, owner decisions 4 Nov, decision A): every custom-hours **create** screen (Book labour, project and small works scheduling, the Daily Overview booking sheet, My Schedule) shows the overtime rate pre-filled with the organisation's rate for that day (`overtimeRateCreateControl({ organization, dayKey, timeZone })` → `{ organizationMultiplier, prefill, options }`), shown to the people who may book (never operatives). `otMultiplierOverride` is stored **only when the person changes it** from the pre-filled organisation rate (`overtimeMultiplierCreateFields({ chosen, organizationMultiplier })`); a booking left at the organisation's rate stores nothing and follows later rate changes, scheduled ones included, and only edited rows are marked on the weekly report (§7). A booking whose day the organisation pays every hour at one rate shows that rate. **Changing or moving a booking** (review fix r274): the Daily Overview booking sheet carries the rate on screen through every save — hours only, another job, Other — and through a move to the other collection (a new document), with `overtimeMultiplierMoveFields({ stored, chosen, organizationMultiplier, newDocument })`; a rate equal to the organisation's is removed, never stored. **Overtime rate on managers' own site bookings** (1.32.0): Scheduling, Daily Overview and My Schedule show a manager site booking's rate with `bookingHoursLabel` like a labour booking (the stored rate, else the organisation's).
- **Edit Booking "Overtime rate"** (Daily Overview and project / small works scheduling, both apps): "Organisation (×1.5)" or "Custom ×__" — `overtimeRateControl({ booking, organization, timeZone })` (shown on a custom-hours booking that has overtime), written with `overtimeMultiplierWriteFields`. Everyone who can edit the booking (`canWriteBooking`: admins, managers on their jobs) may change it, on any person's booking, self-employed included (it changes their timesheet pay); operatives never see Edit Booking.
- **What one change updates.** The booking is the only source: scheduling and My Schedule (times, rate or Cancelled), Daily Overview (`bookingHoursLabel`), warnings (unbooked-labour cover and clashes recalculate; a cog change that makes a clash shows a normal warning and is not blocked), the weekly report PAYE section (§7), self-employed timesheets (§8) and the Overtime list all recalculate from it.

## 26. PAYE price work, expenses and personal site audits (1.30.0)

Owner-approved spec of 4 Nov ("PAYE overtime, price work and expenses", release 1.30; decisions log 4 Nov). `lib/canonical/payeClaims.ts` and `lib/canonical/personalSiteAudits.ts`; the payrun is §4.5 (`payePayrun.ts`); the hub and Users' list are §25 (`overtimeHubAccess`, `overtimeUserSubjects`). Online only. Money is the organisation currency (`resolveOrganizationCurrency`, shown with `formatMoney`). Before 1.32.0 there was no line-manager review; from 1.32.0 there is one (below), and accounts still pay on their side.

- **Where.** Timesheets → Overtime, Expenses & Price Work → My … (PAYE people) → **Price work** / **Expenses**. Each opens on the same three range buttons as Overtime — **Current PAYE payrun**, **Past PAYE payruns** (`payePastPayruns`, with the saved reports of that kind, `payeClaimReportsOfKind`) and **Custom date range** (`payeCustomRange`) — plus **Add** (`PRICE_WORK_ADD_LABEL` "Add Price Work", `EXPENSE_ADD_LABEL` "Add Expense"). Until a PAYE payday is set, `PAYE_PAYDAY_NOT_SET_MESSAGE` and only Custom date range works (and adds). The items of a range are `payeClaimsInRange(items, range)` — an expense by its date, price work when its from–to overlaps the range — oldest first; the list row is `payeClaimListRow` (name and figure; the date and, for price work, the job number and "Approved by …"); the total `payeClaimsTotal`.
- **Price work** (`priceWorkItems/{id}`). The Add Price Work sheet has Cancel at the top and Add Price Work at the bottom. Fields: name (`title`) and description (`details`); **job number** (`jobNumber`, 1.30.1 — see below); dates from–to (`startDate`, `endDate`, organisation-zone day keys; one day allowed); **approved by** — required, picked from `priceWorkApproverCandidates(person, roster, { organizationId })`: the active admins and managers (the people `lineManagerCandidates` offers: never an operative, a pending invite or a deleted profile) whose users document names this organisation and who are admins or carry the manager flag or role (a flagless member is not offered) — the same test as the rules — never the person under any of their ids, no free text; the approver's name is stored beside the id (`approvedByUserId`, `approvedByName`) and the approver is **not notified** (`PAYE_CLAIM_APPROVER_NOT_NOTIFIED_NOTE`); the figure (`amount`, above 0, with `currency`); optional evidence. `priceWorkValidationError` / `priceWorkWriteFields`.
- **Price work needs a job number** (1.30.1, owner decision 4 Nov; `lib/canonical/priceWorkJobNumber.ts`). One rule for PAYE price work and timesheet price work entries (§8): a new item or entry needs a job number — free text like an expense's, trimmed, 1–60 characters (`priceWorkJobNumberError`: blank → `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_REQUIRED_MESSAGE` "Add the job number for this price work.", longer → `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_TOO_LONG_MESSAGE`; `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_MAX` 60). Add Price Work asks for it (`priceWorkValidationError` checks it after the name; `priceWorkWriteFields` writes it trimmed). Items stored before 1.30.1 without one stay valid: `parsePriceWorkDocument` reads `jobNumber` as '' (a stored number is read as text), nothing rejects or rewrites them, and every place the job number is shown — the list row and item detail (`payeClaimListRow`: dates · job number · approver), the report copy (`payeClaimReportSnapshot` now keeps price work's `jobNumber`) and its PDF summary (`payeClaimReportRows`), Users' read-only view — shows `PRICE_WORK_JOB_NUMBER_MISSING_TEXT` ("—") via `priceWorkJobNumberText`. The security rules require it on a new `priceWorkItems` document (string, 1–60); an update of an older item without one passes, and an update that has one keeps 1–60 (§16).
- **Expenses** (`expenseItems/{id}`): the fields of a timesheet expense entry (§8 — `title`, `details`, `jobNumber`, `date`, `amount`) plus `currency` and **at least one piece of evidence** before it can be added (`EXPENSE_EVIDENCE_REQUIRED_MESSAGE`); more are allowed (up to `PAYE_CLAIM_EVIDENCE_MAX`, 20). `expenseValidationError` / `expenseWriteFields`.
- **Evidence** `evidence: [{ path, name, kind, contentType?, auditId? }]` (`parsePayeClaimEvidence`, `payeClaimEvidenceWriteFields`): `kind` `file` | `photo` | `personalSiteAudit`; `path` is a Storage path in the person's own claim folder, `payeClaimEvidencePath` → `organizations/{orgId}/payeClaims/{userId}/{uid}_{ms}.{ext}` (§24 object names; `payeClaimEvidencePathBelongsTo` is checked by the validators). **Readers keep only the person's own claim files**: `parsePayeClaimEvidence(list, { userId, organizationId })`, called by `parsePriceWorkDocument`, `parseExpenseDocument` and `parsePayeClaimReportDocument` with the item's `userId`, drops another person's file, a personal site audit photo, a URL or junk, so a viewer is never offered it. Photos (never SVG) and PDFs, 10MB; web turns a photo into a JPEG so the report can draw it. Apps store the path, never a download URL, so the Storage rules decide who opens it. **Files and §24** (`payeClaimEvidenceToDelete`): when an add is refused or fails after its uploads, the app deletes the files it just uploaded (the uploader's undo window), so no orphaned copy — a frozen personal audit copy included — is left where line managers and admins can read it; the same when a personal audit save fails (its photos). Removing an item deletes its evidence only inside that window; otherwise the files are kept for recovery, as every other removed file (§24).
- **Reports.** **Generate price work report** / **Generate expenses report** turns green once at least one item is in the range (`payeClaimReportReady`). It saves a copy, `payeClaimReportSnapshot` → `payeClaimReports/{id}` `{ userId, userName, companyName, kind, rangeStart, rangeEnd, currency, items: [{ id, title, details, jobNumber, startDate, endDate, approvedByName, amount, evidence }], totals: { amount, itemCount, evidenceCount }, generatedBy, generatedAt }`, written once and never changed (`parsePayeClaimReportDocument`), and builds the PDF from it: the first page is the summary — company name and logo, the person, the range, every item (date · job · name · figure, then its description, approver and evidence) and the total (`payeClaimReportRows`); then **one page per piece of evidence**, its heading (the item's name, date and figure) and details (evidence n of m and its name; the description) at the top (`payeClaimEvidencePages`): a JPEG / PNG drawn as large as the page allows, a PDF's own pages appended after its heading page, anything else listed with `PAYE_CLAIM_EVIDENCE_NOT_DRAWN_NOTE`, and a file that cannot be read with `PAYE_CLAIM_EVIDENCE_UNREADABLE_NOTE` (`payeClaimEvidenceDrawAs`). Several reports per payrun are allowed; the person shares the PDF themselves (web download, iOS share sheet).
- **Who sees what** (`canViewPayeClaims(viewer, subject, roster)`): the person, their active line managers (`lineManagerIds`, the users document) and admins outside operative mode — read-only for everyone but the person (`canWritePayeClaim`: only the person writes). In Users' Overtime, Expenses & Price Work → a person → Price work / Expenses: a PAYE person's items as above, read-only; a **self-employed** person's items are their timesheet entries (§8), read-only, in the same ranges — `timesheetClaimRows(sheets, kind, range)` over `parseTimesheetEntries` of their `settings/timesheet_*` sheets (the revised amount when their line manager edited one; each row's `jobNumber`, shown "—" for price work without one, `priceWorkJobNumberText`, 1.30.1).
- **Review** (1.32.0, owner decisions 4 Nov; `lib/canonical/payeClaimReviews.ts`): the Users' → a person → Price work / Expenses screen matches the overtime review — ✓ or ✕ on each item, one **Approve** button, the message `reviewApprovalMessage` ("Approved 2 of 5. 1 rejected; 1 not decided yet."). Reviewers are `canReviewPayeClaims(viewer, subject, roster)`: the person's active line managers and active admins outside operative mode, never the person under any of their ids. Approve writes one document per decided item in one batch, `payeClaimReviewWrites` → `payeClaimReviews/{kind}_{itemId}` (`payeClaimReviewDocId`) `{ userId, itemId, kind, decision: approved | rejected, fingerprint, reviewedBy, personUserId? }` plus `reviewedAt` and write metadata, read with `parsePayeClaimReviewDocument`. An item's state is `payeClaimReviewState(item, reviews)` — Needs review / Approved / Rejected (`PAYE_CLAIM_REVIEW_STATE_LABELS`) — from the review whose fingerprint (`payeClaimFingerprint`: kind, name, job number, dates, figure, currency, evidence count) is the item's current one, so an item the person edits after a review needs review again. **All records for this person:** items are loaded under every id of the person (`overtimePersonIds`). **1.33.0 (owner, 6 Nov; supersedes decision A for claims): a line manager can always review their report's items, whichever of the person's ids an item is filed under** — there is no "Needs an admin". `payeClaimReviewWrites` writes an item under another of the person's ids (a former invite id or a same-email document) like any other and adds `personUserId` (the person's current users id) to its review, so the security rules can match the line manager through that users document and check the item's id is one of the person's (`formerUserIds` / `sameEmailUserIds`, fields the person cannot change on their own document, §16). Who reviews is unchanged (`canReviewPayeClaims`); `payeClaimReviewOutcomeLabel` only names an item left out as not theirs. A reviewer never writes the item itself (`canWritePayeClaim`). **Review is optional:** the person may generate a price work or expenses report at any time (`payeClaimReportReady`: at least one item in the range); the review informs the accounts team and never gates the report. Self-employed people's timesheet entries keep the timesheet review (§8).
- **Personal site audits** (`personalSiteAudits/{id}`). Site audits has a **Personal** section below Projects and Small works (`PERSONAL_SITE_AUDIT_SECTION_LABEL`) with **Create Personal Site Audit** (`PERSONAL_SITE_AUDIT_CREATE_LABEL`). Job number and job name are free text, not linked to any job, and print on the audit; the audit has a type, an optional title, a date, the author and 1–100 photos each with a title (`personalSiteAuditValidationError`, `personalSiteAuditWriteFields` → `{ ownerUserId, jobNumber, jobName, type, customTitle, date, authorName, items: [{ id, title, location?, comments?, assignee?, annotations?, imagePath, takenAtMs? }] }`; photos at `personalSiteAuditImagePath` → `organizations/{orgId}/personalSiteAudits/{ownerUserId}/{auditId}/{uid}_{ms}.{ext}`). **Visible only to the person who made it** (`canViewPersonalSiteAudit`, `personalSiteAuditsFor`): no admin, manager or line manager reads the list or its photos, and the company data export leaves them out. **Attached as evidence**, the app builds a frozen PDF copy (`personalSiteAuditPdfRows`) and uploads it into the person's claim folder; the evidence entry is `personalSiteAuditEvidence(audit, path)` (`kind: 'personalSiteAudit'`, `auditId`, named `personalSiteAuditTitle`). Line managers and admins see that copy with the claim, never the private list; later changes to the audit never change the copy. Both apps' privacy policy carries `PERSONAL_SITE_AUDIT_PRIVACY_NOTICE` ("Personal site audits are private to the person who created them. A copy attached to an expense or price work item is visible to that person’s line managers and admins, and is kept for recovery if the item is later removed."); the legal pack version is unchanged.
- **Data.** New collections only, nothing existing changes meaning: `priceWorkItems`, `expenseItems`, `payeClaimReports`, `personalSiteAudits` (`LIVE_COLLECTIONS`, `OPERATIONAL_COLLECTION_PATHS`); Storage kinds `paye_claim_evidence`, `personal_site_audit` (§24). The company data export includes the first three; the data clean-up script has a report-only `paye-claims` check (owners who are not people of the organisation, expenses without evidence).

---

## How the rulebook changes

1. A rule change starts in `project-planner-web`: edit the function in `lib/canonical`, add a test in `lib/canonical/canonical.test.ts`, and edit this file.
2. Bump `Version:` (major: a breaking field or behaviour change; minor: a new rule; patch: wording) and add a `### <version> — <date>` entry under Changelog.
3. `npm run build:canonical` packs the script with this file's version stamped in, writes `lib/canonical/dist/canonical-manifest.json`, and mirrors the files into `../project-planner-ios` when it is checked out.
4. `npm run rulebook:handoff` writes the iOS follow-on prompt to `docs/ios-handoff/v<version>.md` and `docs/ios-handoff/LATEST.md`.
5. Enforcement: the pre-commit hook, CI (`.github/workflows/rulebook.yml`) and the Claude Code Stop hook (`.claude/settings.json`) all run `node scripts/rulebook-sync.mjs check` and refuse a change to `lib/canonical`, `firestore.rules`, `storage.rules`, `firestore.indexes.json` or this file without a version bump, a changelog entry, a fresh pack and a hand-off. A daily CI job compares the iOS repo's mirror with this version and fails when iOS is behind.
6. iOS runs `scripts/sync-rulebook-from-web.sh`, and its `RulebookParityTests` fail when its mirror, manifest or packed script differ from what web published.
7. **A write-shape change updates the cross-platform contract** (1.10.0): when an app changes what it writes to a shared collection, or a reader changes what it returns, regenerate `lib/canonical/cross-platform-contract.json` (`npx tsx lib/canonical/crossPlatformContract.samples.ts --write`; web samples come from the web writers, iOS samples are copied from the iOS payload builders named in `iosWriter`) in the same change. `crossPlatformContract.test.ts` fails until it is, and iOS `CrossPlatformContractTests` fails when its builders no longer produce `iosWritten`.
8. A gap found on iOS is reported to the web agent and fixed here first — never by diverging on iOS.

## Deploy notes

**1.32.3** (= 1.32.2 with a third compile-cost cut; no behaviour change): **Firestore rules publish needed** — Firebase console → Firestore → Rules → paste `firestore.rules` → Publish, or `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c` — then check the live release (`docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "After publishing"). Nothing else: no index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes; `minimumIosBuild` unchanged; iOS needs no change (the packed script differs only in the rulebook stamp). It can go out on its own or with the 1.33 rules, which it makes room for.

**1.32.2** (= 1.32.1 plus a second compile-cost cut; **publish this instead of 1.32.1**, which Google refused: console "unknown error", CLI 409 / 503): **Firestore rules publish needed** — Firebase console → Firestore → Rules → paste `firestore.rules` → Publish, or `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`. No behaviour change from 1.32.1, so everything in the 1.32.1 note below still applies (its order, and raising `minimumIosBuild`). No index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes; iOS needs no change (the packed script is unchanged apart from the rulebook stamp).

**1.32.1** (= 1.32.0 plus the compile-cost cut; publish this, not 1.32.0): **Firestore rules deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`): the new `payeClaimReviews` rules (and the catch-all exclusion) and the overtime request decider writing `status` `pending` after a partial approval (§16). No index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes. **`minimumIosBuild` is raised before 1.32 switches on** (owner decision 4 Nov; §18): an older iOS build stores `otMultiplierOverride` on every custom-hours booking it creates differently and approves overtime all-or-nothing, so the owner sets `platformConfig/app.minimumIosBuild` to the first iOS build that ships the 1.32.0 hand-off before announcing 1.32. Order: 1. merge; 2. iOS ships the 1.32.0 hand-off to TestFlight; 3. deploy the rules; 4. web deploy; 5. raise `minimumIosBuild`. Until step 3 a line manager's partial approval and every claim review are refused by the rules (the screens show the refusal).

**1.31.3** (includes 1.31.0–1.31.2, which never published): **Firestore rules publish needed.** Google's compiler refused 1.31.0–1.31.2 (CLI 503, console "unknown error"); 1.31.3 is the same rules with about 17% less compile work than the live 1.30.2. Publish in the Firebase console (Firestore → Rules → paste `firestore.rules` → Publish), or `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`. No index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes. Web is already on 1.31.x. Every 1.31.x behaviour change (rulebook 1.31.0–1.31.2 changelog) takes effect for older iOS builds on publish. If Google still refuses it, the fallback is `docs/rules-cost/1.30.2-plus-critical.rules` (live 1.30.2 plus the critical privilege fixes; see `docs/rules-cost/README.md` for what it leaves open).

**1.30.2**: **Firestore rules deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`): `isTimesheetReviewer` never counts the sheet's subject, the self-signed subject's own write, and the self-manage toggles read for this organisation (`orgSelfManageFlag`) in `hbCallerSelfBooks` and `timesheetSubjectSelfSigns` (§16). No index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes. Order: 1. merge; 2. web deploy (User Timesheets no longer lists the viewer; the review page never offers a counter-signature on one's own sheet); 3. deploy the rules; 4. iOS syncs and ships the 1.30.2 hand-off (iOS also needs its own-sheet review and `InvoicingView` fallback `user.hasLineManager` fixed, and its visitor flags read from the membership). **Who notices after the rules deploy:** an admin who is not self-signed (Timesheet self-sign off, not the Super Admin) can no longer counter-sign, export or write review decisions on their own sheet on any build — their line manager (or the other admins) signs it. On an iOS build before 1.30.2 such an admin's own save that carries `extraEntryReviews` (adding an expense or price work entry saves it as the reviewer's) is refused until iOS ships 1.30.2; self-signed admins and the Super Admin are not affected (their own writes stay allowed for older builds). Visitors: an admin whose toggles are on only in their membership of another company can now self-book / self-sign there; a person whose toggles are on only at home loses them in the other company — check Manage Users there if someone reports "Submit request" where they expected "Submit".

**1.30.1**: **Firestore rules deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`): `priceWorkItems` creates require `jobNumber` (§16). No Storage, index or Functions change; no migration writes — older price work items and timesheet entries without a job number are left as they are and show "—". Order: 1. merge; 2. web deploy and deploy the rules together (web 1.30.1 sends `jobNumber`; a web 1.30.0 page still open would be refused when it adds price work after the rules deploy — reload fixes it); 3. iOS syncs and ships the 1.30.1 hand-off. **iOS:** if an iOS build that writes `priceWorkItems` without `jobNumber` has shipped, its Add Price Work is refused by the rules until it updates (raise `minimumIosBuild`); iOS timesheet saves are never refused for it (no timesheet rule).

**1.30.0**: **Firestore rules and Storage rules deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,storage --project project-planner-f986c`): the `priceWorkItems`, `expenseItems`, `payeClaimReports` and `personalSiteAudits` rules and the catch-all exclusion (§16), and the Storage paths `payeClaims/{userId}/…` and `personalSiteAudits/{ownerUserId}/…` (§24) — without the Storage deploy every evidence and personal audit upload is refused (any other prefix is refused for everyone). No index (the screens query `userId ==` / `ownerUserId ==` only), no Functions change (no new notification types; the approver is not notified), no migration writes. Order: 1. merge; 2. deploy the Firestore and Storage rules; 3. web deploy (Price work, Expenses, Site audits → Personal, the privacy policy sentence, export and clean-up); 4. iOS syncs and ships the 1.30.0 hand-off (until then iOS shows the 1.29 placeholders and no Personal section; nothing it writes changes). Nothing changes for a company until a PAYE person adds an item; until a PAYE payday is set only Custom date range works. The privacy policy gains one sentence with no legal pack version change, so nobody is asked to accept again.

**1.29.0**: **Firestore rules deploy and Functions deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,functions:inbox-push --project project-planner-f986c`): the rules are the line manager's overtime booking update and the `overtimeRequests`, `overtimeReviews` and `overtimeReports` rules (§16); the push function ships its own packed copy of the rulebook (`functions/canonical-business.js`), which must carry the new push types (`overtime_request_submitted`, `overtime_request_reviewed`) and the `overtime` toggle. No index or Storage change; no migration writes. Order: 1. merge; 2. deploy the rules and the functions; 3. web deploy; 4. iOS syncs and ships the 1.29.0 hand-off; 5. raise `minimumIosBuild` to that build **before** anyone uses custom overtime rates or operative overtime requests (an older build prices a custom rate correctly — `otMultiplierOverride` was already read — but cannot show, send or approve requests). **Nothing changes for a company until an admin sets the PAYE payday**, except the weekly report, where PAYE people move from the normal hours and pay rows into the PAYE overtime section. **With `enforcedReads` on**, only admins and the job's managers can approve overtime for people whose line manager does not manage the job (the line manager cannot read those bookings, §16 known limits); with it off (the default today) line managers approve their reports' requests, except lines on bookings filed under an `operatives` row, which need an admin or the job's manager. Only new optional fields and new collections: a missing `otMultiplierOverride` is the organisation rate, signed timesheets are never re-priced, and old bookings simply read as Needs review in a custom range.

**1.28.0**: **Firestore rules deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`); no index, Storage or Functions change; no migration writes. **Behaviour change for admins who have no line manager:** before 1.28.0 every admin without an active line manager was self-approved (own leave saved approved, own timesheet self-signed). From 1.28.0 only the two self-manage toggles (or being the Super Admin) do that. **Before deploying the rules, the owner checks each company:** 1. open Manage Users → Admins (or, after the web deploy, Settings → Data health → "People who need a line manager") and list every admin who is not the Super Admin and has no line manager; 2. for each, either set a line manager, or have another admin turn on Annual Leave Management and Timesheet self-sign (PAYE: Annual Leave Management) in Edit User — the web deploy must be live first for the Timesheet self-sign toggle; 3. then deploy the rules. Order: 1. merge; 2. web deploy (the new toggle, the Data health list, Submit / Submit request and the counter-signature follow the toggles); 3. the owner's check above; 4. deploy the rules; 5. iOS syncs and ships the 1.28.0 hand-off. Until iOS ships, an iOS build on the 1.27.0 script still shows an admin without a line manager as self-approved. For such an admin (not the Super Admin, toggles off) the rules then **refuse** the older build's approved leave save ("Submit" saving `approved`); a non-admin's `weeklyReportOverride` owner save is refused too. The admin's own timesheet snapshot is still accepted because admins are timesheet reviewers in the rules. So do step 3 before step 4, and tell iOS users to update. An admin left with the toggles off and no line manager is not stranded: their leave and timesheets go to the other active admins, the Super Admin included.

**1.27.0**: **Firestore rules deploy and Functions deploy needed**, no index or Storage rules change: `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,functions:inbox-push --project project-planner-f986c` (rules: the invitee's own pay-profile copy and the catalogue-operative pay reads, §16; functions: `sendEmail` `material_request` job checks). 1. merge; 2. deploy the rules and the functions; 3. web deploy (Add User passes the invite draft, the material send route sends `jobId` and checks the job, removed variation evidence is recorded and listed for admins, the privacy policy and DPA carry the retention sentence, the invited rate is copied on accept while `privatePay` is on, operatives managers read catalogue operatives' pay while it is on); 4. **owner, Cloud console**: bucket `project-planner-f986c.firebasestorage.app` → **Protection** → **Soft delete policy** → retention **30 days** → Save (Deploy notes 1.25.0 has the path); 5. iOS syncs and ships the 1.27.0 hand-off. Nothing changes for an organisation while `privatePay` is off, except the material job check, which applies only to requests that send `jobId`.

**1.26.1**: no Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change. Web deploy; iOS syncs and ships the 1.26.1 hand-off (editor seed and save baseline from `payEditorStartingValues`) **before** the owner turns `privatePay` on for any company — with it off nothing changes on either app.

**1.26.0**: **Firestore rules deploy needed** (`firebase deploy --only firestore:rules --project project-planner-f986c`); no index, Storage or Functions change. Nothing switches on by itself: with no `enforcedReadsOrganizationIds` / `privatePayOrganizationIds` and the platform-wide fields missing or false, every read and write is as before. 1. merge; 2. deploy the rules; 3. web deploy (follows both switches live); 4. iOS syncs and ships the 1.26.0 hand-off (plans from the switches, no pay on shared documents while `privatePay` is on, rate history per person, pay profile overlay everywhere pay is shown); 5. raise `minimumIosBuild` to that build; 6. the owner follows `docs/SECURITY_SWITCHES.md`: backfills, then their own company in the lists, checks, then everyone. Rollback: remove the company id from the list (or set the platform-wide field false); no deploy.

**1.25.0**: **Storage rules deploy needed**, no Firestore rules, index or Functions change: `firebase deploy --only storage --project project-planner-f986c`. 1. merge; 2. deploy the Storage rules; 3. web deploy (variation evidence removal deletes the object only when `fileDeletePermission` allows; otherwise it unlinks and keeps it); 4. iOS syncs and calls `fileDeletePermission` before `deleteVariationEvidenceFile` (until then an iOS delete of someone else's evidence is refused and silently ignored — the link is removed, the file kept). 5. **Owner, Cloud console — soft delete** (bucket `project-planner-f986c.firebasestorage.app`): [ ] console.cloud.google.com → project `project-planner-f986c` → Cloud Storage → Buckets → the bucket → **Protection** tab → **Soft delete policy**: confirm it is on and set the retention to **30 days** (1.27.0, owner decision 5; the default is 7, up to 90) → Save. [ ] To restore a file: the bucket → **Objects** tab → switch on **Show deleted data** (or "Show: Live and soft-deleted objects") → find the object by its path (§24) → tick it → **Restore**; or `gcloud storage ls --soft-deleted "gs://project-planner-f986c.firebasestorage.app/organizations/{orgId}/**"` then `gcloud storage restore "gs://…/{path}#{generation}"`. A restored object keeps its path, so the stored download link works again once the record links it. [ ] Optional, longer history of overwritten files: Protection → **Object versioning** on, with a lifecycle rule that deletes noncurrent versions after N days (otherwise every replaced profile photo is kept and billed forever); restore with `gcloud storage cp "gs://…/{path}#{generation}" "gs://…/{path}"`. Soft-deleted and noncurrent bytes are billed as storage. Soft delete also keeps a company's files for the retention period after the §20 eraser runs.

**1.24.0**: no Firestore rules, index or Storage change. **Functions deploy needed** (`sendEmail`: the shared envelope and the optional `jobId` check): `firebase deploy --only functions:inbox-push --project project-planner-f986c`. 1. merge; 2. deploy the functions; 3. web deploy (Add User keeps valid line-manager picks on a type change; the send route uses the shared envelope; email subjects, From, Reply-To and CC are unchanged); 4. iOS syncs: Add User calls `lineManagerSelectionAfterLevelChange` instead of clearing the picks, and the material send may add `jobId`. Until iOS ships, iOS Add User still clears the picks on a type change (the person picks again; nothing is saved differently).

**1.23.1**: no Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change. Web deploy only; iOS follows the 1.23.1 hand-off (weekend window for named slots, its create paths, `userMissingLevel` if it shows a Data health screen). A person with no level who has a `managers` row is no longer treated as a manager on web (pickers, unbooked-labour kind) — the same as iOS today; they appear in Data health to be fixed.

**1.23.0**: no Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change (lists, counts, badges and clash checks are app-side reads; nothing new is written). 1. merge and deploy web; 2. iOS syncs and switches each screen in the hand-off to the 1.23.0 functions. Until iOS ships, the two apps differ only where the hand-off lists it (people lists, Home and badge counts, day boundaries off GB time, Select Operatives on a day after a working-hours change); no stored data changes either way. The setup wizard's weekend Unpaid break can be chosen by a new company from this release: an iOS build older than 1.19.0 prices it as paid (raise `minimumIosBuild` as the 1.19.0 note says if that build is still in use).

**1.21.0**: no Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change (list membership and bookable jobs are app-side reads; writes are still guarded by `canWriteBooking` in the rules). 1. merge and deploy web; 2. iOS syncs and switches every job list, tab, count and picker to the §21 functions (hand-off). Until then iOS still hides inactive small works and counts inactive jobs in no tab.

**1.20.0**: no Firestore rules, index, Storage or Functions change (the rules validate neither `standardPaidHours` nor booking slots). 1. merge and deploy web; 2. iOS syncs and ships the Working hours screen without the Monday–Friday paid-hours box, Full day / Custom booking chips and `leaveHalfDayWindows` for leave. Until then an older iOS build still offers AM / PM (stored and paid as before) and its Working hours save writes a typed `standardPaidHours` without `paidHoursDerived` (that policy is then priced on the typed figure, as before 1.20.0); raise `minimumIosBuild` (§18) once the iOS build is out.

**1.16.0**: 1. merge; 2. `firebase deploy --only functions` (`pushTokenSingleOwner` on users writes; `sendDevicePushOnNotificationCreate` with the shared payload and the actor-token skip) and `firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes` only if `firebase firestore:indexes` does not list `notifications (userId ASC, createdAt DESC)`; 3. `node scripts/dedupe-push-tokens.mjs` (dry run), review, then `--apply`; 4. web deploy (server-time rows, inbox retry, leave conflicts in booking flows); 5. iOS: sync, call `notificationRecipientAccountId` / `bookingCreatedRecipients` with the operatives rows, write `FieldValue.serverTimestamp()` through `notificationCreateFields`, switch company on push tap (`notificationOrganizationSwitch`), and `bookingLeaveConflicts` in its booking flows.

**1.15.0**: 1. merge; 2. `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,firestore:indexes,functions` — the deletion lock and restore rule, the `orgMemberships.organizationId` collection-group index the eraser needs, the `material_request` sender and recipient rules, and the new daily `eraseDeletedOrganizations` function (needs the Cloud Scheduler API; the default service account deletes Storage files and Auth users). **Deploy it with `platformConfig/app.organizationErasureEnabled` missing or false**: it then only logs what it would erase. After reviewing the first dry-run log (Cloud Functions logs, "organisation erasure (dry run, switch off): would erase"), the platform owner sets it true; 3. web deploy (Delete organisation, Restore, Delete my account paths, the material send route); 4. iOS: call `canSendMaterialOrders` / `materialOrderRecipientCheck` on Send and history, the kept toggles on Change user type, the deleted-organisation screen (`organizationDeletionState`), and later Delete my account (`accountDeletionPath`).

**1.13.0 / 1.13.1** (one merge; full order in `docs/DEPLOY_1.11.0.md`, section "1.13.0"). 1. merge; 2. `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` — `payProfiles` is private, the lapsed-subscription gate and the email-claim move are live, `privatePay` stays off so pay reads are as before; 3. web deploy (writes billing access fields on the next Stripe sync, writes pay to both places); 4. release iOS 1.13.1 (same), force it with `minimumIosBuild`; 5. `node scripts/backfill-pay-profiles.mjs` (dry run, then `--apply`); 6. a later release of both apps that reads pay, rate history and timesheets per person, then the platform owner sets `platformConfig/app.privatePay` true; 7. `node scripts/backfill-pay-profiles.mjs --apply --strip` — from here no colleague can read another's rate.

**1.12.0 / 1.12.1** (full order and checks in `docs/DEPLOY_1.11.0.md`, which now covers 1.10.0–1.12.1). The read scoping of 1.10.0 / 1.11.0 waits behind `platformConfig/app.enforcedReads`, so the rules deploy no longer waits for the backfills. 1. merge the web change; confirm in the Firebase Auth console that `info@projectplanner.us` is email-verified (the rules and the owner API require it) and that `platformConfig/app.enforcedReads` is not true; 2. straight away `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,firestore:indexes,functions` — every 1.12.0 security fix and the 1.10.0 / 1.11.0 write rules go live, reads stay as before 1.10.0; 3. release iOS 1.12.0; 4. `node scripts/backfill-job-manager-user-ids.mjs` (dry run, then `--apply`); 5. `node scripts/backfill-booking-job-collection.mjs` (dry run, then `--apply`; again just before step 7); 6. a later release of both apps with `ENFORCED_READS_ACTIVE` true, after steps 4 and 5 report `0 to update` (force iOS with `minimumIosBuild`); 7. the platform owner sets `platformConfig/app.enforcedReads` true — rollback is setting it false, no deploy. Write `platformConfig/app` `{ minimumIosBuild: <build> }` only when a build must be forced.

**1.11.0** (superseded by the 1.12.0 order above; kept for history): `ENFORCED_READS_ACTIVE` stays false in this release. 1. merge the web change (deploys no rules); 2. `node scripts/backfill-job-manager-user-ids.mjs` (dry run, then `--apply`) — before any app build that queries jobs by `managerUserIds` reaches managers; 3. release iOS 1.11.0 (writes `jobCollection` on every booking it saves); 4. `node scripts/backfill-booking-job-collection.mjs` (dry run, then `--apply`; run again just before step 6 to catch bookings older builds saved); 5. a later release of both apps sets `ENFORCED_READS_ACTIVE` true — only after steps 2 and 4; 6. only then `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,firestore:indexes,functions` (indexes may go earlier). Deploying the 1.11.0 rules while the apps still read whole collections refuses every manager's booking lists.

**1.10.0** (full order and checks in `docs/DEPLOY_1.10.0.md`): 1. merge the web change; 2. `node scripts/backfill-job-manager-user-ids.mjs --project <id> --all` (dry run, the default) and review the per-job diff; 3. the same with `--apply` (writes only `managerUserIds`, partial updates, idempotent, no write metadata — `updatedFrom` takes only `web` / `ios` / `android`); 4. `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,firestore:indexes,functions`; 5. release iOS 1.10.0. The read rules (§17) refuse whole-collection job and booking queries for managers without a job toggle and for operatives, so app builds that predate 1.10.0 lose those lists for them until they update.

## Changelog

### 1.32.3 — 2026-11-06
- Rules compile cost cut from 383,790 to 367,346 (static cost, `scripts/rules-cost/metric.py` `total2`; −16,444, −4.3%); no allow/deny change. This makes room for the 1.33 rules changes. The invitation `list`, `create`, `update` and `delete` allows are one allow with a `request.method` test, so `canAdministerInvitation` and `invitationCallerKnown` are checked in one place, and nine helpers that had no `let` are written out where they were called (`canAdministerInvitation`, `invitationCallerKnown`, `legacyMemberRead`, `isOrganizationAdminAnyBilling`, `hbRequesterUid`, `isTimesheetSettingsDoc`, `canChangePayOf`, `hbCallerSelfBooks`, `rosterRowAdmin`). `firestore.rules` 142,995 → 142,906 bytes. Details and where each helper went: `docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "1.32.3". Rules suites 223/223; `RULES_STRICT_BUDGET=1` the same 43 limit refusals as 1.32.2, none new; emulator type check 0 issues.

### 1.32.2 — 2026-11-06
- Compile-cost cut, no behaviour change. Google refused to publish 1.32.1 (console "unknown error", CLI 409 then 503) although 1.31.3 published at a slightly higher static cost, so the margin had gone. Static compile cost (`scripts/rules-cost/metric.py` `total2`) 429,072 → **383,790** (−10.6%; 1.30.2 live 525,898); in-process compile on the emulator's compiler 1.79 s → 1.66 s (min of 7). Every allow/deny decision is unchanged: lazy `let`s for repeated document paths and the caller's users document, `orgIdsMatch` computed once in `userDocOrganizationMatches`, `membersMapAdminAnyBilling` folded into `orgAdminEither`, a redundant `adminLikeUser` removed from `callerOrgRoleAnyBilling`, allows with the same condition merged (clients, tasks, subcontractors, siteAudits) and write allows sharing `canAccessOrganization` merged with a `request.method` ternary (holidayBookings, settings, notifications, overtimeRequests). Details: `docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "Compile cost". Rules suites 223/223; `RULES_STRICT_BUDGET=1` the same 43 limit refusals as 1.32.1, none new; emulator type check 0 issues.
- CI: `scripts/check-rules-budget.mjs` fails when the static compile cost is above `firestoreRulesMaxCompileCost` (395,000, `scripts/rules-budget.json`); the new `rules-typecheck` job (`scripts/check-rules-typecheck.mjs`) fails on any emulator type-check error ("Invalid type" included).

### 1.32.1 — 2026-11-05
- 1.32.0 (never published) on top of 1.31.3's compile-cost restructure, plus one more cut; no behaviour change from 1.32.0. Admin checks that tested the users-document admin and the members-map admin one after the other now read billing once (`orgAdminEither`; `isOrgAdminOrOwner`, memberships, pay profiles, rate history, settings and the other pairs), and `isOrgAdminViaMembersMap` is gone (`membersMapAdminAnyBilling`). Static compile cost 429,072 (1.30.2 live: 525,898; 1.32.0 as built: 614,251); in-process compile 10–17% faster than 1.30.2. Rules suites 223/223; `RULES_STRICT_BUDGET=1` 43 limit refusals vs 45 on 1.32.0, none new; emulator type check clean.

### 1.32.0 — 2026-11-05
- **Overtime multiplier at create** (owner decisions 4 Nov, decision A; §25): every custom-hours create screen shows the rate pre-filled with the organisation's rate for the day (`overtimeRateCreateControl`); `otMultiplierOverride` is stored only when changed (`overtimeMultiplierCreateFields`), so a booking left at the organisation's rate follows later (and scheduled) rate changes.
- **Weekly report multipliers** (§7): PAYE overtime rows per person, job and multiplier, "2h at ×1.5 = 3h" (`overtimeMultiplierText`), rows changed from the organisation rate marked (`changedFromOrganization`, `OVERTIME_RATE_CHANGED_MARK`). Breaking for readers of `weeklyReportPayeOvertime(...).label` (now carries the multiplier).
- **PAYE payday required** (§4.5): saved by the main Save of Payment runs and timesheets, mandatory at organisation setup (`payePaydaySetupError`, `PAYE_PAYDAY_REQUIRED_MESSAGE`), a banner while missing (`payePaydayMissing`, `payePaydayBanner`, `PAYE_PAYDAY_MISSING_BANNER`).
- **Overtime review** (§25): one Approve button (`OVERTIME_APPROVE_LABEL`, `overtimeRequestAnyDecided`); partial approval — `overtimeApprovalWrites` applies what the decider may and returns `lineOutcomes`, `waitingBookingIds`, `appliedBookingIds`, `complete` and `message` (`reviewApprovalMessage`: "Approved 2 of 5…", "Nothing was approved…"); the request stays `pending` until complete; lines under an older operatives-row record show "Needs an admin" (decision A, `OVERTIME_NEEDS_ADMIN_LABEL`, `overtimeLineOutcomeLabel`). A changed booking no longer refuses the whole approval: that line stays waiting. Breaking: the result shape and the `stale` / `needsAdmin` / `refused` / `undecided` refusals are replaced by `nothingApproved` and per-line outcomes.
- **All records for this person** (§25, §26, platform guardrail 1): `overtimePersonIds({ person, roster, operatives })` for overtime loading, sending and approving and for price work and expenses.
- **Future days within the current payrun** (owner option 2, §25): `overtimeEditableUntil` → `canEditOwnBookingOvertime({ editableUntilKey })`; later payruns stay locked.
- **Own overtime edits are requests with a line manager** (§25): `canEditOwnBookingOvertime({ person, roster })` — the Super Admin directly; anyone with an active line manager as a request; anyone without one directly where the rules allow the write.
- **Price work and expenses review** (§26, `payeClaimReviews.ts`): ✓ ✕ per item, one Approve, `canReviewPayeClaims`, `payeClaimReviewWrites`, `payeClaimReviewState`, `payeClaimFingerprint`, `parsePayeClaimReviewDocument`; new collection `payeClaimReviews` (rules §16).
- **Export notice** (§8): `timesheetExportOwnSheetNotice`, `TIMESHEET_EXPORT_OWN_SHEET_NOTICE`.
- **Rules version in Settings** for everyone (§15): `rulebookVersionLabel`.
- **Booking screens follow `canWriteBooking`** (§2, owner decision 4 Nov): `canBookOtherLocationsFor(viewer, personUserIds)` — Office / WFH / Site survey / custom items only for oneself unless admin.
- **Pay visibility narrowed** (§8, owner decisions 5 Nov): `canViewPersonPay(viewer, subject, roster, { context })` — a line manager who is not an admin or an operatives manager sees a report's pay only with `context: 'timesheetReview'`. Read plans keep loading it for the review; firestore.rules pay reads unchanged (needed by the review).
- **Weekly report admin-only** (owner decision 5 Nov, §7, §11): `canOpenWeeklyReport`; `weeklyReports` joins `ADMIN_ONLY_PERMISSION_FIELDS` and its catalogue row is admin-only, so `permissionToggleRows`, `addUserPermissionDefaults`, `lockedPermissionFields` and `effectivePermissions` drop it for managers and operatives; stored values are never rewritten. iOS: hide the Weekly report for non-admins in Home quick actions, the menu and deep links, and drop the toggle from the Manage Users / Add User rows (they come from `permissionToggleRows`).
- **Small shared helpers:** `legalPackAcceptanceWriteFields` (with write metadata), `invitationListConstraints` (both email forms, as stored and lower case, like iOS `InvitationLookup`), `invitationReadErrorIsSkippable` (a clean-up skips only permission-denied invitation reads).
- **Managers' own site bookings** (owner decision 4 Nov): Scheduling, Daily Overview and My Schedule show a manager site booking's overtime rate with `bookingHoursLabel` like a labour booking (the stored `otMultiplierOverride`, else the organisation's). Web already passes it; iOS must pass the stored rate on all three.
- **Review fixes (r274):** decision A holds for keep lines and no-op cogs (`overtimeApprovalWrites` → `needsAdmin` from the booking's person id, whatever the decision); claim reviews are pinned to their item and person in the rules and never written by the person under any id (§16); the weekly report Pay Summary follows `canViewPersonPay` (`reportPayForViewer`, `PAY_HIDDEN_CELL_LABEL`, `PAY_HIDDEN_NOTE`); the Daily Overview booking sheet keeps the rate on every save and move (`overtimeMultiplierMoveFields`). `OVERTIME_APPROVE_REQUEST_LABEL`, `OVERTIME_REQUEST_STALE_MESSAGE`, `OVERTIME_REQUEST_UNDECIDED_MESSAGE` and `overtimeRequestFullyDecided` are deprecated (kept for older iOS builds).
- **Includes 1.31.2** (rules type-check fixes for console/CLI publish; no behaviour change): no function receives a literal `null` (`rosterRowPrivilegeTyped`, `timesheetSame`, `timesheetSameInstant`, `timesheetOwnerLeaves`); see the 1.31.2 entry below.
- **iOS files to change** (hand-off): `Views/Overtime/OvertimeViews.swift`, `Core/FirebaseBackend+Overtime.swift`, `Core/Rulebook+PayeOvertime.swift` (approval result, `overtimePersonIds`, `editableUntilKey`, `person` / `roster`, one Approve); `Views/Overtime/PayeClaimsViews.swift`, `Core/Rulebook+PayeClaims.swift` (claims review); `Core/Rulebook+PayPrivacy.swift`, `Views/ManageUsersView.swift`, `Views/WeeklyReportView.swift` (`canViewPersonPay` context, `reportPayForViewer`, multiplier column); `Views/BookLabourFlowView.swift`, `Views/DailyOverviewView.swift`, `Views/ProjectDetailView.swift` (scheduling), `Views/BookingHoursEditSheet.swift`, `MyScheduleView.swift` (`canBookOtherLocationsFor`, create-time rate, `overtimeMultiplierMoveFields`, managers' site booking rate); `OrganisationInvoicingSettingsView.swift`, `SettingsView.swift` (PAYE payday in the main Save and banner, "Rules x.y.z"); `Core/CustomerLegalPack.swift` (`legalPackAcceptanceWriteFields`); `ManageUsersView.swift` / `UserStore.swift` invitation lookups (`invitationListConstraints`).


### 1.31.3 — 2026-11-05
- Rules restructured for Google's compile limit; no behaviour change. 1.31.0–1.31.2 could not be published (CLI `firebaserules …:test` 503, console "unknown error"): Google's type checker re-resolves every function body at each call site, and 1.31.2 needed about 15% more of that work than the live 1.30.2. 1.31.3 needs about 17% less than 1.30.2 (static cost 431,665 vs 525,898; in-process compile about 13% faster than 1.30.2 and about 30% faster than 1.31.2): booking create / update / delete are one allow per collection, the manager's job is checked twice instead of three times per booking write, organisation-access helpers read each document path once, and 79 small helpers are written out inside their callers (`docs/firestore-rules-notes.md` "Compile cost"). Rules suites 220/220; no new 1,000-expression refusals (`RULES_STRICT_BUDGET=1`: 47 against 49 on 1.31.2); emulator type check clean. Tools and measurements: `scripts/rules-cost/`, `docs/rules-cost/README.md`.

### 1.31.2 — 2026-11-05
- Rules type-check fixes for console/CLI publish; no behaviour change. The Firebase console and the deploy compile reject 1.31.1's `firestore.rules` with three static type errors ("Invalid type. Received one of [null]") because a literal `null` was passed to a function that uses the parameter as a map (`rosterRowPrivilegeTyped`, `managers` and `operatives` rows) or as a time / number (`sameInstantValue` through `timesheetSameInstant`). The `null` branch now sits at the call site (`resource == null ? … : …`), so no function receives a literal `null`; `timesheetSame` and `timesheetOwnerLeaves` follow the same pattern. Every allow / deny decision is unchanged.

### 1.31.1 — 2026-11-04
- firestore.rules slimmed and restructured so it compiles reliably and every `users/{uid}` and `materials` update is decided by the rules, not by Firestore's 1,000-expression limit. The deploy compile (`firebaserules` `:test`) began returning HTTP 503 as the file grew (180 KB at 1.30.2, 188 KB at 1.31.0); it is now 127 KB (comment blocks moved to `docs/firestore-rules-notes.md`, dead and duplicate functions removed, single-use wrappers inlined).
- **Now take effect as 1.31.0 intended** (§11, owner decision 7): an operatives manager's edits of an operative's users document — names and contact, pay (day / hourly rate, pay basis), active flag, employment type and its dates, line manager, leave fields and the operative toggles — and the legacy by-name material claim (an operative claims a material whose `addedBy` is their email or full name). Before, some of these requests were refused because they ran out of expressions, depending on the documents involved.
- **Refused by the rule logic, not the limit:** an operatives manager changing an operative's role, admin / super admin / manager / Operatives flags, self-manage toggles, `hasNoLineManager`, email, organisation or alias ids (`formerUserIds`, `sameEmailUserIds`); a person changing their own pay, line manager, active flag or privileges; anyone editing another person they do not manage; an owned material reassigned or edited by someone else. 1.31.1 also refuses an operatives manager *adding* `email` or alias ids to an operative that had none (1.31.0's field list only looked at changed keys). It also refuses an admin switching their own Annual Leave Management or Timesheet self-sign off through their own profile (§8, 1.28.0: nobody changes their own; the rules' self-edit path let an admin lower them, and in 1.31.0 such requests were refused only by the expression limit). The Super Admin and the organisation's creator still may.
- **Security (second review):** a privilege flag (`isSuperAdmin`, `adminAccess`, `manager`, `operatives`, `annualLeaveSelfBook`, `timesheetSelfSign`) counts as on when it is on in **either** place — the top level or `permissions.{key}` — in every rule that stops a privilege being gained (`privilegeFlag`, `noPrivilegeFlags`), the way `adminLikeUser` already granted admin from either place. Before 1.31.1 an operatives manager could write `permissions.adminAccess: true` onto an operative whose stored top-level `adminAccess` was false (the operative-edit check read the top level first), and an operatives manager could **create** an operative with `permissions.adminAccess` or `manager` / `operatives` set (the create check read only the top-level `adminAccess` and `isSuperAdmin`) — the second was open on the deployed rules. Both are refused, as is a person setting `permissions.isSuperAdmin` on themself. An admin editing their own profile keeps their alias ids (`formerUserIds`, `sameEmailUserIds`) and email link, as everyone else already did. Not changed: the self-edit path still matches a person by their sign-in email without requiring a verified email (an unverified sign-up on an invitee's email can edit the pending placeholder); requiring it would refuse the same-email copies a web profile save writes for every unverified account (no app flow verifies invitees' emails). **Superseded by F5 below** (owner decision 4 Nov: fixed in this release with invitation proof instead of `email_verified`).
- **Security (third review, §16):** (B1) only the organisation's Super Admin creates a users document with `isSuperAdmin` (top level or `permissions`); another admin's create of one is refused. (B2) an operatives manager acts only on a real operative — `managerMayManageOperative` reads the target (the operative level, no admin / Super Admin / manager / Operatives flag in either place, no admin or manager role) for the users update, delete, membership delete, email-claim delete, `operativeDayRateHistory`, `payProfiles` and `operativeProfiles`, and refuses acting on themself. (S1) re-creating your own profile (self-create, re-join, bootstrap) never sets your own pay (`dayRate`, `hourlyRate`, `payBasis`), employment type and its dates, line manager ids or `hasNoLineManager`, unless `members` lists you as admin; listed as an operative you re-create at the operative level only. (S3) an operatives manager creates only a plain operative (no privilege flag, no alias ids, no `hasNoLineManager`, an email with no live claim in the organisation) and writes an email claim only to an operative they manage, replacing only a claim that is empty, names no users document, or names an operative they manage. (S4) an `operatives` row's account link (`userId`, `linkedUserId`, `uid`) is changed only by an admin, or by an operatives manager to an operative they manage; a `managers` row's link by an admin only. Self-delete of your own users document: refused from the fourth review (below); `orgRootCallerIsSuperAdmin` keeps reading the stored flag the way the other grant readers do (reading it in either place would widen a grant).
- **Security (F5, owner decision 4 Nov — fixed now, §11, §16):** accepting an invitation needs proof of the emailed invitation, not only its email. The new `users/{authUid}` that copies a pending placeholder carries the invitation's secret `invitationId` (new field in `USER_DOCUMENT_FIELDS`) and the invitation (for that email and organisation, not used) is marked used in the same batch (`inviteBeingAccepted` via `getAfter`); web `completeInviteSetup` writes both in one batch. The later accept steps — taking the email claim from the placeholder, updating or deleting a pending placeholder (`passwordSet` not true) by email, reading or removing its tax ids, copying its invited pay — need the caller's own account created that way in the placeholder's organisation (it lists the placeholder in `formerUserIds`). The invitee marks an invitation used only with the account or membership written with its id. Before 1.31.1 anyone signed in with an invitee's email (an unverified sign-up was enough) could copy the placeholder, take the claim and act at the invited level. No `email_verified` requirement, so web same-email profile sync is unchanged. iOS has no accept flow of its own (it sends invitees to the web link) and needs no change. Organisation updates by someone with no tie to the organisation, an invitation's own mark-used and own-email claims are now refused by the rule logic, not the expression limit.
- **Security (fourth review, §11, §16):** (B-1) an operatives manager lists, deletes and changes only the invitations they sent (`invitedBy` is them; their queries name it) and creates only an operative invitation in their own name for an email no one else holds (`opsManagerEmailFree`), so they can no longer read an admin invitation's secret id or mint an operative invitation for an admin invitee's email. Accepting an invitation never copies a placeholder above the invitation's level: an operative invitation copies no admin, Super Admin, manager or Operatives flag and no admin / manager role; a manager invitation no admin or Super Admin; nobody accepts a Super Admin (`selfCreateWithinInvitationLevel`). A membership from an invitation takes exactly its role and permissions (unchanged, now judged first).
- **Nobody deletes their own users document** (fourth review NIT, §16): both apps delete accounts through `POST /api/account/delete`, which writes with service credentials; `canDeleteUser` never lets anyone delete themself. The dead rules function `managerOfSameOrg` is gone. An invitation's `invitedBy` names the sender as their users id (web) or their sign-in email (iOS); the rules accept either as the caller.
- **Deploy order (A0):** the web release with this change goes live **before** the rules deploy. An older web build creates the invitee's account without `invitationId` and marks the invitation used on its own, which these rules refuse, so a person accepting an invitation in between is stuck until the new web is live.
- **iOS follow-on (no rule divergence):** (1) `ManageUsersView.swift` (about lines 1160, 4393, 4478) marks old invitations used with a query on `email` alone, which the rules refuse for everyone (no `organizationId`); query `organizationId` + `email`, and as an operatives manager also `invitedBy` (the account's id or email as stored).
